# ODD Task — Phase 9: Consolidation and polish

> **Roadmap authority:** [Standalone Platform Master Roadmap](../../openspec/decisions/standalone-platform-roadmap.md), canonical **Phase 9 — Consolidation and polish** (inserted by the user on 2026-10-01): pay down the debt Phases 5–8 deferred — the SDD/ODD skill rewrite for labdrian's sub-agent team and the deferred cleanup backlog — before building Observability (Phase 10) and Release (Phase 11).

## Problem and why

Phases 5–8 shipped with a recorded backlog of deferred items, an approval baseline that exempts 27 skills failing the lint budget, and skills that were written for a single agent rather than a team of role agents. The user decided the cleanup must not be left out, and set a governing principle: the whole system must align with hexagonal architecture and clean code to the maximum, whatever it costs.

## Inventory

Ultracode workflow `wf_afe65bf2-736` (9 read-only agents) collected 124 items from Engram, the Phase 5–8 ledgers, the skills, and repository state, and verified each against `main` `f19a33c` (Engram #3855). Items proven fixed were removed with their proof (install-hooks summary `28d2082`; workflow store flake `a6d10dc`; #309 and #312 code items; Phase 5 residuals C3/C4; SL2 `--pre`).

## Decisions (user, 2026-10-01)

- **Principle:** hexagonal architecture and clean code to the maximum, whatever it costs; every design fork offers and prefers the maximum-decoupling option (Engram #3856).
- **Q1 hook upgrade:** `install-hooks` replaces owned entries in place when their command changes; a test pins that old unquoted entries are recognized as owned.
- **Q2 upstream core skills:** create new overlay-owned role skills for the sub-agent team; leave the 15 upstream core skills untouched; document them in the vault as sources.
- **Q3 untracked tool directories:** `.gitignore` `.agents/`, `.claude/skills/`, `.pi/`, `skills-lock.json`; resolve the tracked `skills/archify` symlink to untracked content.
- **Q4 #293 frozen promoted pages:** doctor-only report with the explicit-promote / pin remedy.
- **Q5 #312 registry/engine coupling:** the skills domain reads its own registry model through a port; the YAML file is an adapter; tolerant reader with must-understand fields; versioned adapters for format changes.
- **Q6 profile retirement:** snapshot the resolved profile into the workflow log at creation; workflows are self-contained; the catalog only serves new workflows.
- **Q7 runtime adapters:** archive `runtime-roster` as superseded; implement R-141 as an `Adapter` port with a self-registering adapter registry; an unregistered target is an error.
- **Q8 project identity:** a `ProjectIdentity` port with ordered adapters (explicit `--project-id`, origin remote read from `.git/config` without running git, directory name). (The "verb to declare project-scoped skills (R-046/R-047)" first written here was a misreading: R-046/R-047 of `skill-project-scope` are identity rules, superseded by this chain on 2026-10-06, and project-scoped skills are declared with `skills project-register`, Phase 8.)
- **Q9 open SDD changes:** archive `overlay-versioned-releases`; finish `longterm-mem-knowledge-ingestion` as an adapter behind an ingestion port, before the skills documentation.
- **Q10 orphan branches:** delete `pr137`, `feat/archive-reconcile-guard`, `fix/ci-full-history-for-engine-tests` (superseded on main); keep `wip/audit-remediation-snapshot` (review it) and `sdd/shared-project-vault` (input for the vault work).
- **Q11 additions:** `.gitattributes` `skills/** -text`; a deadline on the Pi subprocess through an injected `CommandRunner` port; a real `memory:procedural-skills` signal through a project-context port; a guarded sandbox test of the Pi global tier. OpenCode `skills` stays `partial`.
- **Q12 goal-v2 open question:** close as superseded.
- **Vault (user):** the skills analysis lives in the project vault `~/labdrian-brain` (registered in `~/.labdrian-overlay/vaults.json`): one page per skill and per role, comparisons, open questions; research and thinking happen there; the new skills are designed from that knowledge. The user's uncommitted `.obsidian/` changes are never touched.

## Scope and constraints

- Hexagonal principle governs every task. TDD strict (`go test` from `engine/` and `longterm-mem/`). RDD on. One slice at a time. Candidates under about 150 KB.
- Tests never touch real state. Destructive, GitHub, deploy, vault-write, and `~/.codex` steps each need explicit approval at the time.
- Ultracode: the user opted into multi-agent workflows for this phase.

## Unclear (investigate inside the tasks)

Per-class degradation warnings (sources disagree); the Phase 8 non-blocking findings triage; branch counts; whether the gentle-ai sync overwrites `~/.claude/skills`; `wip/audit-remediation-snapshot` content vs main.

R3-001: the `append:` wrapping of an unsafe component and of a zero store is pinned. R3-1 and R3-2: a 2 MiB record parses, and the append verb accepts a record of exactly `MaxRecordBytes` and `roles resume` loads it back. R3-002: the `LoadChain` documentation states that an oversized record fails the whole chain closed and why no record this program wrote can be one, and a chain with a record at the bound loads and grows. R2: the `presence` package comment moved to `doc.go` (the clause already sat directly under its comment, so this is the only reading that changes anything), and its source guard covers `doc.go`.
  - **H9, second half** (`eea7845`). `capability.CheckEvidence(catalog, declaration)` is pure and tested with an in-memory fake catalog; `capability.TestCatalog` is the port the domain owns. `capabilitytest.NewCatalog(fs.FS)` is the `go/parser` adapter: it reads through an injected `fs.FS` (`os.DirFS` in the guards, `fstest.MapFS` in its own tests), parses each directory once, and cannot reach outside its tree. `TestDeclaredEvidenceExists` stays in `capabilitytest`.   - **Follow-ups settled** (`cf79559`). `R2-magic-readfile-limit`, the case that applied: the write path did bound the record, but only by the CLI-wide 4 MiB stdin cap above the port; `ClearanceStore.Put` itself did not, and `Get` read with a bare `0` (a 512 MiB file allocated 1.28 GB). So the bound is the domain's now, as `roles.MaxRecordBytes` is: `shaper.MaxRecordBytes` is 4 MiB (exactly the cap every stored record was accepted under, so none becomes unreadable), `ParseRecord` refuses a larger record with `ErrRecordTooLarge` (which makes `Put` refuse it by construction), the record verb reads stdin through the same constant (same value, same message), and `Get` reads one byte past it and refuses a larger file naming the size and the bound. Both sides are tested (exactly the bound is stored, read back, and verified by `assess`; one byte more is refused; a sparse 512 MiB file is refused after reading one byte past the bound). One observable consequence, no product decision: a file of more than 4 MiB that something else left in the store is now an unreadable store (`unavailable`), where it was read whole and then refused by the parse (`refused`) or, if it happened to be a valid record, accepted; no record this program wrote can be that large. `R3-1`: `lookUpClearance` is pinned from `cmd` over six states of the store (absent for no store and for the directories without the record; unavailable for a directory, a symlink, a state home that is a file, and an unusable environment) and for a subject that could not be derived. `R3-2`: the wording of a record write failure (`clearance store: atomicfile: create temporary file: ...`) is pinned (skipped as root). `R3-3`: `IsSHA256Hex` has its own table. `R2-duplicated-isolation-diverges`: the `fsadapter` `TestMain` checks every `Setenv` and refuses to run when the isolation cannot be set up. `R2-presence-doc-source-wording` and `R2-roles-test-unexplained-slack-factor`: the comments now say what `doc.go` is for in the presence guard and why the allocation limit is eight times the bound. **Declined: `R4-1`** (the doubled `role chain store:` prefix in `Append` errors): it is the original store's wording (the old `ChainStore.Append` wrapped `LoadChain`'s own-prefixed errors, `chain_store.go` before `4320ab7`), H7 kept every message byte for byte, and changing it alters what `roles append` prints for a script to match; a cosmetic cleanup is its own decision.

Mutations killed: the Service (a differing file treated as persisted, the archive counted, the dedupe key, all stores read before any is persisted, a missing receipt an error in the survey, no port check, the hook listing the stores before it knows there is a change), the adapter (common store ignored, git dir guessed from the root, mode 0644, absent receipt a plain error, shapes in the other order, files counted as changes, a missing store an error, no folder created, a relative root not made absolute) and `gitprov.Locate` (HEAD required, the checks of `Observe` skipped, the environment not scrubbed).
  - **The hook cwd.** The target document's caveat does not hold: the hook runs `review-receipt hook --cwd "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-$PWD}"`, the directory the session started in, which can be any directory of the repository, and `capture --cwd` is whatever the caller passes. `gitprov.Observe` refuses a root that is not the toplevel, so the adapter uses the new `gitprov.Locate`: it asks git for the toplevel from the directory (scrubbed environment, fixed argv), then judges that toplevel with every check `Observe` makes, but does not need `HEAD` to name a commit (a repository with no commit yet has git directories all the same, and the old code served it; `Head` is left empty). Tested with the fake runner and a real git: the same stores from the toplevel and from a directory inside it, in a plain repository and in a linked worktree. `openspec/` stays under the directory as given, so the behavior of a hook started below its project is unchanged and pinned: with no `openspec/` there it passes through and captures nothing, with its own it captures there. **Finding for the owner, not changed:** that pass-through is a silent fail-open for a session started in a subdirectory of the project (the receipt is not captured, the acknowledgement burns it, and the archive gate later blocks with "no verified receipt"); making the hook look at the toplevel's `openspec/` is a behavior change and a product decision.

A throwaway driver ran 47 scripted scenarios against the pre-move binary (`2343584`) and the post-move binary, each in a fresh world at identical absolute paths, comparing exit code, stdout, stderr and the tree under the world with modes and digests: 37 identical, 10 different. The driver was validated first: the old binary against itself showed no difference (after normalizing the random temporary names), and two broken builds each added their own differences (the hook allowing the ambiguous change: 3 scenarios; the dedupe off: 1). The 10, all edge cases: (a) git's words when git finds no repository (3 scenarios: the hook with one and with two active changes, the capture verb); (b) a store path in an error is symlink-resolved when reached through a symlink (1); (c) a lineage id with a slash fails to write in `atomicfile`'s words instead of `rename`'s (1), and a dangling symlink at a destination, which the old rename replaced, is now refused (`atomicfile` never replaces a symlink) (1); (d) layouts `gitprov` refuses and the old code trusted (5): `GIT_DIR` in the environment, a forged gitfile (the old code captured from the repository it named), a bare repository, a working directory inside the git directory, and `git init --separate-git-dir` without `core.worktree`. The driver is not kept.
  - **Needs the owner's decision.** The fifth class is a tightening of a fail-closed hook: in those layouts the acknowledgement is now denied, with gitprov's words saying why (for the separate git directory, `core.worktree` is not usable; setting it fixes it). It is the cost of `gitprov` being the one owner of git resolution. The alternative is a more lenient `Locate` (skip the gitfile checks), which weakens the one posture `gitprov` has; not taken.

- **Batch 7 decision** (user, 2026-10-02): keep `gitprov` strict for the review-receipt hook (option A). Layouts `gitprov` refuses (`GIT_DIR` in the environment, a forged gitfile, a bare repository, a cwd inside `.git`, a `--separate-git-dir` worktree without `core.worktree`) now get a fail-closed deny with a reason, where the old code trusted them; one owner of git resolution, one posture. Open product follow-up: a session started in a subdirectory with no `openspec/` still passes silently without capturing (unchanged from before).

Carried over from the old code (`reviewreceipt.go:147-150` at the base), fixed because the hook fails closed (decision A).   - **Follow-ups settled** (`b4e7722`). `R1-unvalidated-path-join` and `R3-capture-change-whitespace`: `reviewreceipt.CheckPathComponent(kind, name)` is a pure domain rule (not empty, not `.` or `..`, no `/` or `\`, no white space at either end, no control character; the same on every platform) returning an `*UnsafeNameError` that names the name and the rule. The Service applies it to the change name in `Capture` and in the survey, and to the file name of every approved receipt in the one walk of the stores, before the sink is asked anything; a blank change name still says "change name is required". The hook denies on it (`review-receipt: capture failed: reviewreceipt: receipt file name "../../escaped.review-state.json" is not a safe path component: it holds a path separator`), what came before the refusal stays captured, and `capture --change ../x` exits 1 naming it. Observable consequence: a change directory whose name has white space at an end can no longer be captured into (the hook denies); none exists. `R2-1`: `reviewreceipt/receipttest` (ring `support`, imports nothing of the receipt) holds the one `StateDocument` and `ReceiptDocument`;   - **D4 differences** (the contract lists use the strict parser: an inline list `[a, b]`, brackets required; the refusal reads `malformed <key>: expected an inline list such as [a, b], got "<value>"`). Each is pinned by golden cases that change in `c892bc3` and no others (23 of 40 goldens change; the 17 that do not are the valid and odd-shaped documents, the injection points, the work-context matrix, the embedded contract, the registry states, the refusals of an input or of a missing file or flag, and the OpenCode runs that were not lenient). (1) `applies_to_phases` or `excluded_phases` without brackets (`sdd-tasks, sdd-apply`): was two phases, is refused. (2) An unclosed or unopened bracket (`[sdd-tasks, sdd-apply`): was accepted, is refused. (3) `excluded_phases:` with no value: was an empty list, is refused. (4) Text after the closing bracket (`[a, b] # note`): was accepted with the note in the last item, is refused. (5) Quoted items (`["sdd-tasks", 'sdd-apply']`): were accepted with the quotes kept in the names, so they never matched a phase; the quotes are stripped now, so they match. (6) `applies_to_phases:` with no value: refused before ("missing or empty") and now; only the words change. (7) The context metadata and `context_operator`, which only the gate and the OpenCode adapter read (the gate skipped such a contract silently): a malformed `language_context` or `activation_context`, or any `context_operator`, is refused by every reader now. `gate-task` still passes the prompt through and now says why on stderr; `propagate` exits 1 (it wrote the row before); the status check reports FAIL (was OK); `runtime install --target opencode` refuses a minimalism contract with one (was installed with the metadata ignored), and still skips such an OO contract. For an OpenCode OO contract, (1) to (4) now skip it where it used to be included. A refused contract is passed through by the gate, so it no longer strips either. No contract in use is affected: the three contracts under `skills/_shared`, the embedded one and every test fixture use bracketed lists. Not changed: the OpenCode entry of the minimalism contract still carries no context metadata, whatever the file says, as before; the Pi gate (`engine/pipkg/labdrian-gate.ts`) already read these lists strictly, so Go now agrees with it, and its comments still cite the old `gate.go` line numbers (left alone: editing the embedded asset changes the Pi package bytes; a follow-up for H26, which splits `pi.go`).

  - **Pins before the move** (`dd7cb6d`, `bae20fc`, `a6831da`). `cmd/hook_golden_test.go` and its case files record, from the unchanged program, the exit calls, stdout and stderr of `gate-task`, `projection hook --event UserPromptSubmit`, `projection hook --event PreToolUse`, `skills guard-hook` and `shaper guard-hook`: 55 golden files under `cmd/testdata/hook-golden`, each its own subtest and file, and one more case, `hook-reads-the-input-it-names-and-nothing-else` (21 inputs), in `review-receipt-golden`, where the review-receipt hook's allow and deny paths were already pinned. Every hook is fed every shape it cannot use (empty, truncated, an array, `null`, two objects, a field of the wrong type, keys in another case, a key twice, a reader that fails), its input at and one byte over its bound (1 MiB for both projection events, 4 MiB read by `gate-task`, 8 MiB for the approve guard, none for the clearance guard and the receipt hook), a recovered panic and a stdout that cannot be written. Run-dependent text (temporary paths, event digests) is replaced, and `-count=3` is identical. What the pins found, and the move therefore had to keep: each hook decides for itself what to do with an input it cannot use (the clearance guard denies, with the decoder's own words; the approve guard and the receipt hook let the call through; `gate-task` passes it through); each reads only its own fields (`gate-task` ignores `tool_name`, the receipt hook ignores a `file_path` of the wrong type beside the command, the clearance guard accepts `null`); `gate-task` writes `updatedInput` with the HTML escapes of `json.Marshal` and every other reply without them, and echoes the four fields of the Agent tool in the order of its struct. Load-bearing (mutants of the old code, `mutate_pins`, throwaway): 39 mutants, 35 killed. The four that survive: the cleaning of the input's `cwd` (not observable through a verb, because the repository key normalizes the path; pinned at the decoder in slice 1a), a warning in the other event's shape (equivalent: both shapes print `{"systemMessage":...}` for a bare warning), skipping the relevance check of the gate (equivalent in bytes, because `Gate` allows what it has no opinion on; the property that the hook never opens the stores for such a tool is `TestPreToolUseTouchesNoStoreForAToolTheGateDoesNotCheck`), and the sentence of a denial with no reason (unreachable through a verb; pinned in 1b).
  - **H14** (`4aed1fb`, `c2e7b5a`, `d1a3531`, `f5b2025`). **Design.** `engine/hookwire` (ring adapter) imports nothing of the module and is the one home of the hook's JSON tags; each policy owns the typed values it takes and returns, and `cmd/hook_translate.go` maps between them. Maximum decoupling, chosen over a shared domain package: a shared type couples five policies to one package and to the vocabulary of one runtime, while policy-owned values and an adapter that knows no policy mean a Codex or Pi adapter can feed the same policies without importing Claude's. `projection.GateInput` has `EditTools` (the four tools, supplied by `cmd` as `gatedEditTools`; with none, the gate gates no edit) and a `ToolCall` with the name and `QueryArguments` (`Named`, `Project`); `projection.PanicWarning` takes an `Occasion` instead of an event name; `GateResult.Explanation` keeps the sentence for a denial with no reason; `gate.Rewrite(Call, Config)` replaces `Process`; `shaper.DecideGuard(GuardCall)` and `GuardUnreadable(detail)` (fails closed); `skills.DecideApproveGuard(ApproveGuardCall)`; `reviewreceipt.Service.CheckCommand(command) Verdict`; the approve guard's 8 MiB bound, a bound on bytes of the wire, is `cmd`'s. Deleted: `projection` (`HookInput`, `ParseHookInput`, `PreToolUseInput`, `ParsePreToolUseInput`, `decodeHookObject`, `cleanHookCwd`, the event constants, `MaxHookInputBytes`, the output structs and both `*Output` methods, `EditTools`), `gate` (`Process`, the four wire structs), `shaper.RunGuardHook`, `skills` (the input struct and bound), `reviewreceipt.RunHook` and its struct. **The decoders are several, not one,** on purpose: each reads exactly the fields its consumer read and no others, because a decoder that read the union would refuse inputs that one hook accepts (a Bash call whose `file_path` is a number, beside the acknowledgement the receipt hook guards, would stop it capturing, and a `cwd` of the wrong type would make `gate-task` stop injecting); the golden files pin each accept set. They share the one set of tags in one package. **Two compatibilities kept byte for byte, to be decided by an owner, not by this unit:** the HTML-escaping asymmetry of `gate-task` (`UpdatedInput` is written as `json.Marshal` writes it, the other replies are not), and the clearance guard's denial for a shape error, which carries `encoding/json`'s words including the type they name (`shaper.guardHookInput`, `Go struct field guardHookInput.tool_input of type struct { ... }`): `hookwire.legacyDetail` and the view's name `guardHookInput` exist for that, `TestToolCallDetailsAreTheWordsTheClearanceGuardAlwaysPrinted` pins 22 inputs, and deleting `legacyDetail` is the one-line change. Tests moved with their code: the decoders and encoders to `hookwire` (`decode_test.go`, `encode_test.go`, `guard_test.go`), the settings-matcher tests to `cmd` (`gate_tools_test.go`, cited there by `capability/declarations.go`), the gate's whole-chain tests to `cmd/gate_task_e2e_test.go` (same names), the gate and projection tests onto the typed values.

H14 had no `knownDebt` line (the guard reads imports, and `encoding/json` is pure), so the unit carries its own fitness tests: the ring row `hookwire` (RED: `package hookwire has no ring: declare it in rings` and `cmd -> hookwire breaks the dependency rule`), `TestNoHookWireFormatOutsideHookwire` (reads the module and fails on a tag of the protocol outside `hookwire`; its table `owedWire` had six rows at birth, each owed to a commit, and each row was deleted first, RED each time: `projection/context.go holds a tag of the hook protocol outside engine/hookwire`, `gate/gate.go ...`, then `reviewreceipt/hook.go`, `shaper/guard.go` and `skills/approve_guard.go`; the table is gone) and `TestHookwireImportsNothingOfTheModule`.

This entry is the commit `docs(odd): record Phase C batch 10`, named by its subject because a commit cannot name its own hash.
  - **Follow-ups settled** (`b704e3e`; 40 findings, 34 applied, 6 declined). **The two bounds.** `R4-shaper-guard-unbounded-read`, `R3-shaper-guard-unbounded-read-documented`, `R3-agentcall-unbounded-decode`: `hookwire.MaxToolCallBytes` (8 MiB, the bound of both guards; a caller reads one byte more to see it was passed) and `hookwire.MaxAgentCallBytes` (4 MiB, the bytes `gate-task` has always read) are named limits like `MaxEnvelopeBytes`, and a decoder over its bound returns an error wrapping `ErrTooLarge`. The approve guard keeps its bytes and its fail-open (an input over the bound is let through, as one it cannot read is); the clearance guard fails closed and denies an input over the bound with its usual words and the reason. The golden `shaper-guard-reads-without-a-size-bound` is replaced by `shaper-guard-judges-input-up-to-eight-mebibytes` (its two cases byte for byte, plus the bound and what is over it). **`cmd/hook_translate.go`.** `R3-hook-translate-no-unit-tests`: `hook_translate_test.go` (268 lines) tests the mapping in both directions; `R4-gatecall-unconditional-query-parse`: `projection.ToolCall.ReadQuery` is a lazy reader, called by the gate only for a memory query and at most once, pinned by `TestTheArgumentsOfACallAreReadOnlyForAMemoryQuery` (a file edit's input is no longer decoded for a project it does not name); `R3-hookwire-updatedinput-error-unproven`: the encode failure branch of `agentGateAnswer` is reached through an `encode` parameter and tested; `R2-duplicated-passthrough-fallback` (one fallback), `R4-gatereply-unconditional-fallback` (`gateReply` explains only a denial), `R2-002` (the call is built once), `R4-newline-coupling` (a test holds every answer to one trailing line break). **The restored precision.** `R2-wire-invariant-test-removed` and `R3-approve-guard-bound-precision-test-removed`: the whole-chain absence of `hookSpecificOutput` for a missing prompt is `TestE2E_MissingPrompt_LeavesNoHookSpecificOutputAtAll` in `cmd/gate_task_e2e_test.go`, and a command that really matches `skills approve`, padded past the bound, is allowed because of the bound and not because of its content in `TestSkillsGuardHook_AMatchingCallOverTheBoundIsAllowedBecauseOfTheBound` (`cmd/skills_guard_test.go`), each at the package that owns the code now. **The compatibility rewrite.** `R2-legacydetail-fragile-type-name-match`, `R3-legacydetail-blanket-replace`, `R4-002`, `R4-001`: `hookwire.ClearanceGuardDetail(err)` is for the one guard that shows the decoder's words, tells an error that is not the decoder's (the size refusal) in its own words, and finds the type name by the type (`reflect`), not by a copy of its text, so renaming the type changes no byte of the denial; `TestTheClearanceGuardsWordingStillRewritesWhatTheDecoderSays` fails if the decoder stops naming it as the rewrite expects, and `TestToolCallDetailsAreTheWordsTheClearanceGuardAlwaysPrinted` (22 inputs) still passes byte for byte. **The rest, applied.** `R3-gated-edit-tools-mutable` and `R1-001` (`gatedEditTools()` is a function returning a list of the caller's own, held to the four tools and to the documentation by `gate_tools_test.go`; the gate's rule that with no list it gates no edit was already said at the field `GateInput.EditTools` and in the package doc, and stays), `R3-global-fault-seam-mutation` (the guard's fault seam is a parameter), `R3-panic-non-string-coverage`, `R2-decideapproveguard-name-collision` (`approveGuardVerdictFor`), `R2-comment-misattributes-json-absorption`, `R2-001` and `R3-002` (two header comments), `R2-duplicated-bash-closure` (`bashToolCall`), `R2-gateTask-duplicates-gateTaskReader`, `R2-stub-no-missing-file-signal` (a path that is not in the stub does not exist), `R2-shaper-guard-let-through-mislabel` (two cases named for what they do), `R3-hidden-line-separator` and `R3-001` (the golden harness writes a character that reorders or hides text as `\uNNNN`, so no golden holds a raw U+202E or U+2028 any more; two goldens changed in representation only), `R3-002` (a new golden, `pretooluse-cuts-a-project-name-at-two-hundred-characters`: 199, 200, 201 characters and 201 multi-byte ones), `R3-001` of `contract` and `R4-bracket-validation-silent-skip` (the refusal of a bracket inside a quoted item is the strict list rule of decision D4, kept, and now pinned by `contract_test.go` and `list_internal_test.go`), `R3-key-matching-inconsistency` (documented in the package doc of `hookwire`: three decoders match a key without regard to case and `Query` matches `project` exactly, each pinned, none changed to agree without an owner). **Declined, with the reason:** `R2-exitreply-stderr-method-naming` (`Stderr()` is the bytes of the stderr half of the exit-code reply, named for the protocol it speaks like `Block` and `Message`; a rename moves five call sites for no reading gain); `R2-generic-label-reuse` (each run of a golden prints its own command line, which is what tells the ten cases apart; a label per case would restate the arguments); `R3-realistic-roundtrip-coverage-reduced` (the encoding half of that test is `hookwire/encode_test.go` and the shapes end to end are the projection hook goldens; recomposing the old test would couple `projection` to the encoder again); `R3-receipt-hook-textual-lookalike-match` (the hook recognizes the phrase by its text on purpose, so that a heredoc is caught, and the golden pins it; changing the match is a change to the hook's contract); `R4-approve-guard-silent-size-skip` (the approve guard's fail-open is the designed behavior, kept byte for byte, and a warning for an input over 8 MiB is a change to a hook's output for an owner to decide, like the wording and the escaping below); `R4-passthrough-diagnostics` (`gate-task` passes through silently by design and every one of its goldens says so; a diagnostic is an output change). **Not touched, by instruction:** the clearance-guard denial wording, `gate-task`'s HTML escaping, and the OpenCode loader's primary-contract abort.
  - **H15 pins before the move** (`23097e8`, `c83bef1`). `cmd/registry_golden_*_test.go` and 58 golden files under `cmd/testdata/registry-golden` (about 95 KB), recorded from the unchanged program, each its own subtest and file: what `skills list`, `status`, `validate`, `add`, `remove`, `sync-manifest`, `install`, `adopt`, the project verbs, `pipkg build` and `check` and the Pi runtime adapter print and leave, over every state of a registry (not there, a key the reader does not know, a version it does not know, a value outside its vocabulary), over 29 invalid entries, the forms of YAML the subset allows and every refusal of the rest. Run-dependent text is masked (`<WORLD>`, digests) and `-count=3` is identical. Load-bearing: 82 mutants of the old reader, writer, verbs and `pipkg`, 79 killed; the three that survive are equivalent or dead (an entry with an empty path never reaches the writer; a global skill has no projects, so the admission test refuses it anyway; encode then decode is equal for everything the program can build); 13 mutants did not compile and were redone, and two survivors of the first round (the superseded-by of `project-status`, the cross-check of an already divergent manifest) were killed by cases added afterwards.
  - **H15** (`a4a55df`, `0547627+ad82e9e`, `099674d`, `4f9c3da`). **Design.** `skills.RegistryRepository` (`Load`, `Decode`, `Encode`), owned by the domain, implemented by `skills/registryyaml` (ring adapter; imports only `skills`, a test says so); `Registry.Validate` is pure and applied by the domain (`ReadRegistry`, `DecodeRegistry`), and a test reads the adapter's package and fails on any call or name of a judgment in it. Maximum decoupling, chosen over leaving `Parse` in `skills` behind an interface: the adapter knows the file and nothing of what a registry means, so a second format (or a test double) needs no domain change. **The order contract of the port,** which is why the goldens did not change: the file was always read entry by entry with each judged as soon as it was whole, so a decoder that fails hands back the entries that were whole before the fault, the domain validates them first, and only then reports the fault of the reading; a mis-indented line that ends an entry early therefore reports the invalid entry it leaves, not the stray line. An unreadable store is a `*skills.RegistryReadError` and returns the zero registry. **Moved.** `parse.go` and `serialize.go` to `registryyaml/decode.go` and `encode.go` (history kept), their tests and testdata with them (`skills:TestSerializeRoundTripRealRegistry` is `registryyaml:TestEncodeRoundTripRealRegistry`, `skills:TestSerializeGoldenTwoEntry` is `registryyaml:TestEncodeGoldenTwoEntry`; the citation in `openspec/decisions/standalone-platform-roadmap.md` is fixed); the vocabulary of a registry (`ScopeGlobal`, `SourceCore`, ...) and `RegistryTargets()` live in the domain. **Call sites.** Every verb of `skills` (`list`, `status`, `skills`, `lifecycle`, `sync`, `install`, `project_cli`, `match`) takes the repository after `readFile`; `RenderListCore` and `RenderStatusCore` take it instead of `readFile`; `pipkg` and the Pi runtime adapter read through the port they are given (a file- or directory-backed registry); `cmd/registry_repository.go` constructs the adapter and is the one place that does. The tests of package `skills` cannot import the adapter (an import cycle in a test), so `export_test.go` and an external `skills_test` file register it when the test binary starts. **Closed by H15:** the `parse` and `serialize` call sites, `pipkg-registry-direct-parse`. The two checks the adapter keeps because they name a line (a scope outside its two words, a source that contradicts its type) are checks of the file, worded with the line they are on, and the domain holds every registry to the same rules again.
  - **H16** (`1045cce`; Q5, no new decision). **Closes #312.** The file's `version` selects the decoder (`decoders`, one per version, with the field table of each in `schemas`); the reader is tolerant of what it does not know; a fixed set of fields is never read without being understood. **The version rule.** The version is found first (the value of the top-level key `version`; the last one if the file says it twice, as before), a version no decoder is for is refused naming it (`skills: version "2" is not supported; only version "1" is valid`, and `the valid versions are "1", "2"` when there are several) before anything else is read, and a file with none is refused for that before any fault of its entries. A change to the format that a reader of the version before would misread is a new version with a new decoder beside the old ones; a change that only adds a field is not. **Unknown fields.** A key the reader does not know, in any mapping, is left out and said: `Registry.Unread` holds one note per field (`line 7: unknown key "mirror" in source`), what is under it is skipped, and a key that repeats inside a mapping is still refused as a duplicate. The verbs that read warn on stderr (`warning: registry fields left unread: <first> (and N more)`, so a script that reads stdout is not changed) and go on; `AddEntry`, `RemoveEntry` (first thing they check) and the adapter's `Encode` refuse a registry that has one, because writing it back would drop it. `pipkg` reads the registry too and does not warn: it builds from `id`, `path` and `install.targets`, all of the set. **The must-understand set of version 1,** computed from the schema table the decoder reads (`registryyaml.MustUnderstand("1")`), found by reading what consumes each field, and written with one line of rationale per field at the top of `registryyaml/doc.go` (a test holds that list to the code): `version` selects the decoder; `skills` the entries (a value there is not an empty list); `id` names the skill in the install directory and the project lock; `path` is the directory the skill is read from and the containment boundary of install and `pipkg`; `source` holds `source.type`; `source.type` decides the manifest tag (managed or custom), so how `sync-manifest` and `validate` treat the skill; `install` holds the scope, the projects and the targets; `install.defaultScope` is where it is installed, and a scope read as none skips the approval check (`approval_gate.go`); `install.allowedProjects` are the projects `install` admits a project skill to, and read as empty admits it to none; `install.targets` are the runtimes it is projected to (the Pi package, `list`). Not in the set, because nothing decides on them: `source.upstream` and its owner, `source.repo`, `source.ref` (provenance, which only the domain's rule on the source type looks at) and `lifecycle` with `updateStrategy` (shown by `list`, required by the domain's rule). **The shape rule.** A field is read in its own shape (a scalar has no block under it; a block or a list has no value on its line). A field of the set in another shape is refused, naming the field and the line (`line 5: source must be a block of fields (type, upstream, repo, ref), not a value`); any other field has the wrong part left out and said (the block under a scalar is skipped; the value on the line of a block is noted and the block under it is read, as it always was without a word). **Every behavior difference from the strict reader,** each pinned: (1) a key the reader does not know is read around and said (was a refusal; 330 of the generated documents), at the root, in an entry, a source, an upstream, an install and a lifecycle; (2) `add` and `remove` refuse such a registry, naming what would be lost; (3) the version is judged before the entries: an invalid entry before a version the file gets wrong now names the version, a file with no version and a mis-indented entry names the missing version, and a file whose first line is indented, or a byte-order mark before `version`, says the version is missing (was `unexpected indentation at document root` and `unknown top-level key "﻿version"`); (4) `version:` with no value says `version "" is not supported` (was `unknown top-level key "version"`); (5) a field of the set in a shape it does not read is refused by name: `skills: nope` (was `unknown top-level key "skills"`), and an inline value on the line of `source`, `install`, `install.targets` or `install.allowedProjects`, which the strict reader ignored while it read the block under it, is now refused (139 documents the old reader accepted, none of any other kind), and a block under `version`, `id`, `path`, `source.type` or `install.defaultScope` is refused by name (was `unexpected indentation`); (6) the value on the line of `source.upstream` or `lifecycle` is read as before and now noted (35 documents), and a block under `source.upstream.owner`, `source.repo`, `source.ref` or `lifecycle.updateStrategy` is left out and noted (was `unexpected indentation`), after which the domain still refuses what it requires (an owner for a core skill's upstream, a repository for an external source, an update strategy); (7) consequences of (1) in the words of a refusal: a source with no type but a stray key, a lifecycle with no strategy but a stray key, an upstream with only a stray key, and an unknown key before an invalid scope now name what the domain finds, not the stray key; (8) an unknown key that repeats at the root is told twice, because the strict reader never checked the root for a key that repeats (the same quirk as `version` and `skills` twice, kept: the last wins). **Goldens changed: 19 files (34 case blocks) and 4 new.** Thirteen files hold the state of the harness "a registry with a key the reader does not know" (`mirror: x` in a source), which every verb meets: the same input now exits 0 with the warning for `list`, `status`, `install`, `adopt`, `sync-manifest`, `project-register`, `-retire`, `-status`, `pipkg build` and `check` and the Pi adapter, and refuses to write for `add` and `remove`; the helper is renamed `registryStates` (it is not a list of refusals any more) and its files keep the directory name `refused/`, so the paths in the goldens stay. Six files are the cases of the reader (the unknown-key golden, renamed `registry-leaves-out-keys-it-does-not-know-and-refuses-keys-that-repeat`, 8 of 13 cases; `registry-refuses-a-document-that-is-not-shaped-like-one`, 2 cases; the byte-order mark; the version with no value; `no type of source` and `no update strategy in a lifecycle`; `an unknown key, then an invalid scope`). The four new, in `registry_golden_policy_test.go`, over the registry of every kind of entry: `registry-names-its-version-before-it-reads-its-entries` (8 cases), `registry-says-what-it-leaves-out` (5), `registry-refuses-a-field-the-install-depends-on-in-a-shape-it-does-not-read` (10, one per field of the set that can have the wrong shape), `registry-leaves-out-a-field-nothing-depends-on-in-a-shape-it-does-not-read` (6). Review receipt toplevel.** The change is in the `fsstore` adapter, not in the domain: `Store` finds `openspec/` under the toplevel `gitprov.Locate` reports, asked once and shared by the four ports; it keeps the root as given when that is the toplevel (paths printed keep their form), when git refuses or reports none, and when the toplevel has no `openspec/changes` but the directory has its own (judgment call, see below). `review-receipt capture` uses the same store, so it follows. RED on the old store: four golden cases and four unit tests. Goldens: `hook-in-a-subdirectory-looks-for-openspec-there` (which pinned the bug) renamed `hook-in-a-subdirectory-captures-into-the-toplevel` and now captures; new `hook-in-a-subdirectory-names-paths-under-the-toplevel`, `...-of-a-linked-worktree-captures-into-its-toplevel`, `...-prefers-the-toplevels-openspec`, `...-serves-its-own-openspec-when-the-toplevel-has-none` and `...-of-a-repository-without-openspec-passes`; every other golden is unchanged. Mutation: removing the fallback failed the golden and two unit tests. **Judgment call for the owner:** the old test `TestAHookStartedInsideTheRepositoryLooksForOpenspecWhereItStarted` pinned that a subdirectory with its own `openspec/` is served by it; a pure toplevel rule would silently drop that case, so it falls back only when the toplevel has none, and the toplevel wins when both have one. Removing the fallback is one `if` in `locateOpenspecRoot`.

Q8's order has the origin remote between the two, so with it a repository whose origin is github.com/acme/demo would be `github.com/acme/demo` instead of `demo`, and a registry that admits a skill to `demo` would admit it to nobody there. So `cmd.newProjectIdentity` chains the explicit id, `GitOrigin` and the directory name, in the order Q8 gives. The writer built and pinned `GitOrigin` and left it out of the chain, because putting it in changes the id of a checkout with an origin (`github.com/acme/demo` where it was `demo`) and a registry that admits a skill by directory name; the owner chose to put it in (2026-10-06, option A).   - **What moved.** `engine/skills/app` is the first application-ring package (`archguard.Application`, row in the architecture test): `ListSkills`, `RegistryStatus`, `LintSkill`, `LintRuleTable` and `ValidateOverlay`, with typed inputs, the domain's ports, typed results and typed errors (`RegistryError`, `FileReadError`, `ManifestReadError`, `ManifestParseError`, `ScanError`, `ErrSourceRootRequired`). The CLI adapter is `cmd/skills_cli.go` (reads the arguments, prints, exits) and the one parser `cmd/skills_flags.go` (a `skillsFlagSpec` per verb: value flags, switches, the flags the wrapper appends to every verb, how many words, `--`). `cmd.runSkillsCore` sends `list`, `status`, `lint` and `validate` to the adapter and every other verb to `skills.SkillsCoreAt`. Deleted from the domain: `RenderListCore`, `RenderStatusCore`, `RenderLintCore`, `RenderValidateCore`, `RenderList`, `RenderStatus`, `parseRegistryFlag`, the dispatcher's retry loop and the tests of all of them (their cases live in `skills/app`, the adapter's tests, the parser's table and the goldens). `RenderLintRules` stays in the domain: it is the one generator the style guide's contract tests hold byte for byte.
  - **Locks without a verb.** `AcquireLocks` (returns `*LockError`: not configured, registry unreadable, busy, unavailable, each with its words and exit code), `ReadConsistently` (the shared read that is made again when the lock file appears during it; `*ReadRaceError`) and `OverlayLocks` (the policy of which verb takes which overlay lock, one place: a test holds it equal to the dispatcher's) are exported by the domain; the dispatcher prints their errors in the same words as before. Validate's reads and the typed API are held by `lock_acquire_test.go` and `cmd/skills_validate_test.go` (the first-writer interleaving, the bound, the inspection failure, every read under the lock).
  - **ApprovalRecordStore.** `ReadSkill(sourceRoot, path)` and `ReadRecord(sourceRoot, id)`, owned by the domain (`SkillMDPath` and `ApprovalRecordPath` say where), implemented by `skillsfs.Approvals`, wired in `Deps.Approvals`; `ReadApprovalStatus` and `CheckApprovals` take it in place of `readFile`, and `add`, `approve` and (until it moved) `validate` refuse when none is wired. **Not done:** `ProjectLockStore`. It replaces the five lock reads of the project verbs and of install and adopt and the read-gate seams of the lock tests, so it moves with those verbs; doing it here would change their signatures twice. `AddCore` and `RenderApproveCore` take the store now and move with their group.
  - **Pins** (`cmd/skills_argv_golden_test.go`, 29 cases, one subtest each, recorded from the unchanged program): the flags each read-only verb takes, a flag given twice (the last wins), a flag with no value (last: unset; before another flag: that flag is its value), unknown flags, the equals form, words that are no flag, the verb after the flags (`skills --registry list` names the registry `list`), lint's `--rules`, wrapper flags, extra path and `--`.
  - **D4 differences** (one CHANGELOG line, under Unreleased / Changed): `list`, `status` and `validate` refuse a flag they do not name (`--frobnicate`, `-v`, and the equals form `--registry=path`) with `error: skills <verb>: unknown flag "<flag>"` and exit 1, before taking a lock or reading anything; they ignored it. `lint` already refused it. One more, found by the parser's tests and not covered by any golden: `lint "" b.md` used to lint `b.md` (an empty first word did not count as a path); it now refuses the second path like any other. Goldens changed, each read in its diff: `registry-golden/list-prints-the-entries-sorted-by-id` (the case that said "other arguments are not read" now has the words run alone and the unknown flag refused), and in `skills-argv-golden` the `*-argv-says-what-it-does-with-a-flag-it-does-not-know` and `*-argv-finds-the-verb-after-the-flags` cases of list, status and validate. Every other golden is untouched; the flags the wrapper appends (`--registry`, `--manifest`, `--source-root`) are still accepted by every verb, which a parser that read only its own flags would have broken for list, status and lint. Words that are no flag are still ignored by list, status and validate (pinned; D4 speaks of flags).

Mutations: a program that hangs without starting the descendant fails (`did not say it held the FIFO`), and without `ownProcessGroup` the descendant outlives the program and the test fails (`still held the FIFO after the program was killed`). (Note: `readyWait` was later split into `programReadyWait` and `descendantGoneWait` in `016803c`.)
  - **Suggestions of the group after that (4, none declined), all in `016803c`.** `R2-001` (symlink check): the self-linking HEAD is its own test, `TestGitOriginSaysItCannotTellWhenTheHeadLinksToItself`, which calls `t.Skip` with the reason when `os.Symlink` fails. `R2-002` (one wait): `readyWait` is two named waits with their comments, `programReadyWait` and `descendantGoneWait`. `R3-fifo-reader-leak` and `R4-001`: every way out of the FIFO test ends in the cleanups registered right after each resource, which run in reverse: cancel the context, close the test's write end, kill the descendant by pid and the program's process group, read a second FIFO (the witness, held open by the program and its descendant) to EOF, close the read end so the reader goroutine returns, and wait for the goroutines (a `WaitGroup`, bounded). Mutations: closing the reader before the program starts fires the reader error path and leaves one failure (the intended one), no `sleep` process and no goroutine (`pgrep -x sleep` is 0, the witness and goroutine checks are silent); a cleanup without the context cancel and the kill reports a goroutine still running and leaves a process; without `ownProcessGroup` the descendant still fails the test.
  - **Suggestions of group 6 (2, none declined).** `R2-001` (ledger): the FIFO line above now notes that `readyWait` was split into `programReadyWait` and `descendantGoneWait` in `016803c`, history untouched. `R2-002` (witness cleanup): the dependency is removed, not commented: the cleanup goroutine is counted with `goroutines.Add(1)` in the same step as its cleanup is registered, so the wait for the goroutines needs no place in the order; the redundant second close is gone, one `witness.Close()` after the wait, which also ends a read still waiting on a timeout. Mutation: a goroutine that never calls `Done` is reported by the wait (\"a goroutine of the test was still running\"); the FIFO test passes ten times under `-race`.
  - **Group 7 (2 suggestions and a defect the parent found, none declined).** The defect, proven by the parent (`scratchpad/fifoclose/main.go`): on a FIFO made blocking with `SetNonblock(false)`, `os.File.Close` blocks while another goroutine has a read pending and does not end the read ("Close did not return in 2s", "Read still blocked after Close"). So the witness cleanup of `ca5f0db` would hang the test forever, instead of failing it, when the descendant survived, the reader's cleanup `Close` would hang the same way, and the comments that said closing ends a pending read were false. `R3-001` (the dependency only moved) and `R2-001`: the `WaitGroup` is gone. Each goroutine owns its file and its signal: the reader goroutine reads the program's ready line and then the end of the FIFO, closes the reader itself once its reads returned and closes `readerDone`; the witness cleanup owns the goroutine that reads the witness and closes the witness only after that read returned; the run of the program has `runnerDone`. A cleanup waits for a signal with `awaitDone` (bounded by `descendantGoneWait`), reports what outlived the wait and leaves that goroutine blocked, never closing under a read. The cleanups run in the order cancel and wait for the run, close the write end, kill, read the witness, wait for the reader goroutine. Every remaining goroutine is one of these three; none is counted outside the goroutine's owner. Mutations: with the kill cleanup and `ownProcessGroup` removed and both waits at 3 s, the test fails within the bounds (three reports: the descendant held the FIFO, the program or its descendant still running, the reader goroutine still running) and does not hang (run under `timeout 120`); the reader error path (the first read replaced by an error) fails once, with the injected error, and leaves nothing behind. `-race -count=10` passes.
  - **Group 8 (defect found by the independent verifier after the owner skipped the native review of `dab4058`), `e03c74b`.** With `sleep 3600 &` removed from the script (no descendant ever started) the FIFO test passed in 38 of 40 runs: the program still wrote `ready`, `wait` returned at once, the program exited and the FIFO ended with no descendant ever holding it; the pid file was read only by a cleanup. Now, after the ready line and before the kill, the test requires that the program has not ended (`runnerDone` open), that a descendant pid and the program's own pid were recorded, that `kill(pid, 0)` succeeds and that `Getpgid(pid)` is the program's group. Mutation (the same, `-count=40`): 40 of 40 fail (`the program did not record its descendant (pid 0)`, once `the program had ended when it was to hang`); the real test passes `-race -count=20`.
  - **Suggestions of group 9, `dcfece9`.** `R2-001`: the checks before the kill are a named helper, `requireAHangingProgramWithALiveDescendant`. `R3-001`: the pid files are complete when the helper reads them, because the script writes both pids before the ready line, one command after the other, and the program cannot end between the checks because it waits for the descendant, which only the test kills; the helper's comment says so. Mutation (no descendant, `-count=10`): 10 of 10 fail; the real test passes `-race -count=20`.

  - **What moved.** `skills/project_register.go` became `project_plan.go` (the types of a plan, the commit orders), `project_plan_guards.go` (the write guard, decision (f), the identity rules), `project_plan_steps.go` (the steps the three planners share), `project_register_plan.go`, `project_revise_plan.go`, `project_retire_plan.go`, `project_fs.go` (the `ProjectFS` port), `project_exec.go` (the executors), `project_stager.go`, `project_retire_stager.go`, `project_destinations.go`; the line multiset of the new files is that of the old file (checked by a script, banners and imports aside), and the same for the test file, which became `project_register_plan_test.go`, `project_exec_fixtures_test.go`, `project_exec_test.go`, `project_revise_test.go`, `project_retire_test.go`. The planners are linear lists of named steps (`requireProjectRoot`, `requireDraftOutsideRoot`, `requireNoCarriageReturn`, `readDraftFrontmatter`, `checkSkillID`, `requireRecordedSkill`, `requireAgentOwned`, `planRegisterTargets`, `planRegisterLock`, `planReviseTargets`, `planReviseLock`, `planRetireDeletions`, `planRetireLock`); the verb is a parameter of the shared steps, so the three sentences that differed only in the word are one function. `writeGuard{root, resolve}.destination(rel)` replaced `resolveWritePath(RegisterInput, ...)`, which carried a resolver in a registration's input for install, adopt, revise and retire. The stagers: `stage`, `commit`, `commitOne`, and the three steps of a rollback (`restoreReachedDestinations`, `removeLeftoverTemps`, `removeCreatedDirectories`); the retirement is `projectRetireStager.run` (`stageLock`, `removeTargets`, `commitLock`); `checkProjectDestinations` is a `destinationProof` that resolves the root and the project's `skills/` once and checks each write. `planSkill` is a `skillPlanner` (`inspectDir`, `readRecordedFiles`, `planSourceFiles`, `planDroppedFiles`) in `install_plan_skill.go`; `SyncManifest` is `changesFromManifest`, `partitionAroundAnchor`, `skillBlock`, `assembleManifest`, `checkRegeneration`; `prepareInstall` no longer exists (H18 made it `app.runPlanned`), which is now `admittedSources`, `readSources` and the plan. **Typed result.** The executors take no stream: `ExecuteProjectPlan`, `ExecuteProjectRetirePlan`, `ExecuteProjectRevisePlan` and `ExecuteInstallPlan` return an error, and an execution that failed and could not put everything back returns `*skills.RollbackIncompleteError{Verb, Unrestored, Cause}` (the sentinel `ErrRollbackIncomplete` is found in it, the text is the old one); `app.ExecutionError{Err, Unrestored}` replaces `Report` and `cmd` prints `error: rollback incomplete: <path>` for each, before the error, as it always did. The `wrote:` and `removed:` lines the executors wrote to a stream nobody read are gone (the use cases told the paths from the plan already); their tests now hold `ProjectCommitOrder` and `ProjectRetireCommitOrder`. **`pathguard`.** `WithinRoot`, `Resolver` (the port) and `ResolvedWithinRootUsing` stay; `fsresolve.KeepingMissing` and `fsresolve.ResolvedWithinRoot` are the adapter. The skills wrappers (`withinRoot`, `resolvePathKeepingMissing`, `resolvedWithinRoot`, `resolvedWithinRootUsing`) and their duplicate tests are deleted; the one test of `skills/pathguard_test.go` that is about `PlanInstall` moved to `install_plan_test.go`; `zero_fetch_test.go` lost its `pathguardDebtImports`.
  - **Behavior change (one, a message).** `pipkg build` resolves the destination with `fsresolve`: a destination reached through a symlink with no target, a loop of links or an unreadable directory is refused before anything is built, `pipkg: resolving destination: ...`. The old resolver read such a link as absent; the build then did all its work in a temporary directory and failed at its last step (`creating ...: file exists`, `swapping built package into place: ... not a directory`); exit code and the tree left are the same (thirteen forms of the destination, old binary against new: five differ, the five refused forms above, and only in the words). One CHANGELOG line. No other byte of any program output changed.

- **Phase D batch 18 (H23 = C18).** Branch `refactor/phase9-b18` from `58f656c`; seven commits plus this entry. The runtimes are built through a `runtime.Registry` of `Factory func(Config) Adapter`: each adapter exports its own registration (`RegisterClaude`, `RegisterOpenCode`, `RegisterCodex`, `RegisterPi(r, registries)`), the composition root (`cmd/runtime_registry.go`) calls them in the order `--target all` acts, and an unregistered target is an error (`Registry.New`, `Registry.Parse`); no `init()` and no package-level registry. The target vocabulary is `capability`'s (`capability.IsTarget`, `Targets`): `runtime.Target` constants are defined from it, `Registry.Register` refuses a name outside it, and `skills` validates `install.targets` against it. `NewFoundationAdapter`, `foundationAdapter`, `ParseTarget`, `ExpandTarget` and `runtimeAdapterForTarget` are deleted. HOME, XDG_CONFIG_HOME, CODEX_HOME, OVERLAY_DIR and STATE_DIR are read once in `cmd` (`runtimeConfigFromEnv`) and handed down in `runtime.Config`; no adapter reads them (a source scan test fails on `os.UserHomeDir` or on `os.Getenv` of those names in `engine/runtime`). `knownDebt` stays empty.
  - **Commits (lines / files).** `ecd1f3d` `capability.IsTarget` (+28 -3 / 2); `3bdd074` `Config`, `Registry`, the `Register*` functions, the totality test (+586 -133 / 6); `614ccc5` `cmd` builds through the registry, `--config-root` reaches pi (+189 -24 / 4); `5038a05` the old parser, expansion and foundation adapter deleted, their tests moved (+36 -165 / 5); `86dbb54` the adapters take their directories from `Config` and read no environment, `PiPaths`, `NewLongtermMemAdapter(cfg, ...)` (+279 -225 / 18); `b7b07cc` `skills` validates against `capability` (+96 -70 / 20, 15 of them goldens); `9ac752b` pi's package under the config root, not in it (+79 -38 / 5). Review groups (each far under 3,000 lines and 45 files): A = `ecd1f3d`, `3bdd074`, `614ccc5` (the registry and the switch: +803 -160, 12 files); B = `5038a05`, `86dbb54` (the deletions and the environment removed from the adapters: +315 -390, 22 files); C = `b7b07cc`, `9ac752b` (skills vocabulary, the pi rule: +175 -108, 24 files, 15 of them one-line goldens).
  - **What moved.** `runtime/runtime.go` lost the parser, the expansion, the foundation adapter and `DefaultClaudeConfigRoot`; `runtime/config.go` holds `Config` and the pure rules for the default roots (a relative `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or `CODEX_HOME` is ignored, no home gives no default); `runtime/registry.go` holds `Factory`, `Registry` and the `Register*` functions; `DefaultCodexConfigRoot`, `DefaultOpenCodeConfigRoot`, `DefaultLongtermMemStateDir`, `DefaultLongtermMemBinaryPath`, `DefaultClaudeMCPConfigPath`, `DefaultPiPackageDir`, `resolveHome` and the env reads of `NewPiAdapter` are replaced by `Config` methods; `NewPiAdapter(registries, PiPaths{Home, OverlayRoot, RegistryPath, DestDir})` replaces `NewPiAdapter` and `NewPiAdapterWithPaths`; `skills/registry_validate.go` lost `registryTargets`, `RegistryTargets` and `isRegistryTarget`; `skills/lifecycle.go` names its default targets with `capability`'s constants. `skills` gained a module-internal import (`capability`, pure, held by `TestZeroFetchCoversCapabilityImports` like `pathguard` and `jsonstrict`).
  - **Behavior changes (two, CHANGELOG, both flagged for the owner).** (1) The `--config-root` fix: `runtime <action> --target pi --config-root <dir>` keeps the Pi package at `<dir>/pi/labdrian-pi` (the way `$STATE_DIR` does) where it was dropped. **Decision made by the writer: the package is under the root, not the root itself.** With the root itself as the package directory (the meaning `longterm-mem register --target pi --config-root` has) `--target all --config-root X` gives X to Claude, Codex, OpenCode and Pi at once, and the Pi build swaps X for the built package and `pi remove` plus `RemoveAll` delete it: the comparison below showed `X/settings.json` of Claude gone after `uninstall --target all`. Under the root the other three are untouched (test and comparison). The alternative is the root itself, for the single-target form only. (2) The refusal of an unknown `install.targets` value lists the runtimes in `capability`'s order, `claude, codex, pi, opencode`, where it listed `claude, opencode, codex, pi` (15 goldens, one line each). **Decision made by the writer:** two orders could not both survive one vocabulary; `capability`'s order is pinned by the JSON of `runtime capabilities`, its usage line and the README, so the error text moved. The other choice is to reorder `capability.Targets` to `claude, opencode, codex, pi` (the order of `--target all` and of `bin/labdrian-overlay`) and change that JSON and its refusal instead.
  - **Owner decisions (2026-10-08).** Both behavior changes are kept as implemented: (1) with `--config-root` the Pi package sits at `<root>/pi/labdrian-pi`, not in the root itself, because a Pi build replaces its whole directory and would remove the other runtimes' files under a shared root with `--target all`; (2) the refusal of an unknown `install.targets` value lists the runtimes in `capability`'s order (`claude, codex, pi, opencode`), and `capability.Targets` keeps its order. No code change.

- **Phase D batch 19 (H24 = C10, the Pi `CommandRunner` part).** Branch `refactor/phase9-b19` from `63c021c`; six commits plus this entry. The Pi adapter reaches the `pi` CLI and the package build only through two ports it owns, `CommandRunner` (`LookPath`, `Run(ctx, bin, args...)`) and `PackageBuilder` (`Build`, `Check` returning the disclosure), given in `PiPorts` with `PiOptions{SkipSubagents, CommandTimeout}`; `NewPiAdapter(ports, paths, options)`. Tests: `runtime/fake_commands_test.go` (the fake runner, recording program, arguments and deadline), `runtime/pi_commands_test.go`, `cmd/pi_commands_test.go`, `cmd/runtime_pi_wiring_test.go`; the stub-script helper `writeStubPiScript` and the `LABDRIAN_PI_BIN` pins are gone.
  - **Decision on `LABDRIAN_PI_BIN` (made by the writer, no stop).** It is a test seam, not an interface: its own doc said "tests use it", and the whole repository (bin/, docs, README, CHANGELOG, scripts, TUI) names it only in Go tests and in archived records of the change that introduced it. So it is removed, not moved into `Config`. A person who had exported it will find `pi` on the `PATH` instead; the CHANGELOG says so. `LABDRIAN_PI_SKIP_SUBAGENTS` is documented in the README as an opt-out, so it stays as configuration read once in `cmd`; `LABDRIAN_PI_DEPLOY_REF` is used by the shelltests and recommended for CI, so it stays the same way.
  - **Behavior change (one, CHANGELOG, flagged for the owner).** Every `pi` command now runs under a deadline, `DefaultPiCommandTimeout` = 5 minutes, where there was none: a command still running is stopped (`context deadline exceeded (signal: killed)`) and the step is reported partial like any failing command. **Value chosen by the writer:** `pi install npm:...` fetches a package over the network, so it is minutes; five is generous for it and for `pi install <path>` and `pi remove`, and finite. It is a constant with a field in `PiOptions` for a test, not a flag or a variable. The owner may pick another value, or want it configurable.
  - **Owner decision (2026-10-08).** The five-minute deadline stays fixed (`DefaultPiCommandTimeout`), as implemented; making it configurable is a small change if a slow network ever needs it. No code change.
