- **Phase D batch 20 (H25: `pipkg` `SourceRepo`, the shared SKILL.md frontmatter reader, the file split; and the six carried suggestions).** Branch `refactor/phase9-b20` from `2156d0e`; nine commits plus this entry. The package builder asks git only through `pipkg.SourceRepo` (`IsWorkTree`, `HasCommit`, `HasPath`, `IsAncestor`, `Resolve`, `HasChanges`, `LatestTag`, `Export`); the four yes-or-no questions return `(bool, error)` (since `5ea3a2c`, below): git answering no, or not being installed, is a `false`, as the inline `exec.Command(...).Run() != nil` always was, and a git that could not answer is an error. The adapter is the new `pipkg/gitsource` (`Repo`, over a `Runner`, with `Options{Env, Timeout}`), the `Runner` is `execrunner.Runner.Output` (new: the two streams apart, an explicit whole environment, `WaitDelay`), and `cmd/pipkg_source.go` wires them once for `pipkg build|check` and for the Pi runtime. `pipkg` imports no `os/exec` (`TestThePackageStartsNoProcess` reads the imports) and no environment (the older scan). `pipkg.NoRepository` is the source of a tree that is not under version control. `Build` and `Check` are methods of `Packages{Registries, Source, Options}` (`Compare` returns the `CheckReport`, `Check` the disclosure the Pi port wants). `skills.ReadFrontmatter` returns the top-level keys of a SKILL.md or agent file (value as written, `Text()` unquoted, the number of list items under each) and a typed `*FrontmatterError` for a missing fence; it shares `splitFences` with `SplitSkillFile`, so there is one place that decides where a frontmatter is, and it stays apart from the lint's field reader and from `engine/contract`. `pipkg` and the Pi runtime use it and keep the words of their refusals. `knownDebt` stays empty; one ring added (`pipkg/gitsource`, adapter).
  - **Commits (lines / files).** Carried: `fb82c49` piguard scan (+162 -19 / 2), `6b9b8f0` runtime allowlist key and doc wrap (+24 -11 / 3), `a6411c8` execrunner timing bound (+30 -6 / 1). H25: `2bf8a4f` split of `pipkg.go`, declarations moved whole (+1087 -1033 / 8); `a864567` `skills.ReadFrontmatter` (+266 -18 / 3); `9a08211` `execrunner.Output` (+115 -5 / 2); `ca5da52` `SourceRepo`, `NoRepository`, `gitsource` and its ring (+514 / 4); `54f6686` the frontmatter switch in `pipkg` and `runtime/pi.go` (+216 -62 / 4); `d710b56` the git switch, `Packages` methods, `cmd` wiring, every test moved (+729 -248 / 30). Review groups (contiguous, each far under 3,000 lines and 45 files): A = `fb82c49..a6411c8` (the carried fixes, 6 files, about 250 lines); B = `2bf8a4f` (the split alone, move-only: `git diff --color-moved` shows no edited line; 8 files, 2,120 lines); C = `a864567..ca5da52` (the three additions, 9 files, about 920 lines); D = `54f6686..` the ledger commit (the two switches and the documents, about 1,300 lines and 37 files).
  - **The six carried suggestions, none declined.** `R2-recordingT-fatalf` and `R3-003-recordingT-fatal` (`fb82c49`): `CheckTestSources` takes `piguard.Reporter` (`Helper`, `Errorf`), which `*testing.T` satisfies, and the test recorder implements exactly those two, so there is no nil embedded `testing.TB` to panic on and no `Fatalf`; the `sprintf` wrapper is gone. `R3-002-scan-gaps` (`fb82c49`): the scan now finds the call under the name the file gave `os/exec` (an alias) and a program named by a package string constant, and a file that does not import `os/exec` is not read; what it still cannot see (a variable, a function result, a constant inside a function, joined strings) is in the doc and pinned by `TestCheckTestSourcesStatesItsLimits`, so widening the scan shows as a test to rewrite. Mutations: alias ignored, constants ignored and the name hardcoded to `exec` each fail a test; a mutation that skipped the "imports os/exec" check changed nothing, so the guard was equivalent and is deleted. `R2-allowlist-key-format` (`6b9b8f0`): the key is `environmentRead{file, variable}`, and the doc says the scan does not follow a function-level constant that shadows a package one (no file of `engine/runtime` declares one). Mutations: the variable of the entry changed, the file of the entry changed, and the key built with a wrong file each fail the scan. `R2-doc-line-wrap` (`6b9b8f0`): the doc of the guard test in `runtime` and in `cmd` is wrapped evenly, no logic. `R3-001-timing-bound` (`a6411c8`): the bound is `killGrace + 5s`, the sleep lasts 30s and a pid file lets the test kill it at cleanup (`ps` shows no `sleep 30` left). Mutations: `WaitDelay` removed, `Run took 30.00s`; `WaitDelay` of 8s, `Run took 8.2s, want it back within 7s`.
  - **What moved where.** `pipkg.go` (1123 lines) became `pipkg.go` (package doc, `Options`, `Packages`, `loadRegistry`), `manifest.go` (the embedded gate, the manifest types and the `mcp.json` constants), `build.go`, `check.go`, `comparison.go` (where the comparison tree comes from), `provenance.go` (the commit and version a build records), `extract_tar.go`, `tree.go`. The git calls of `resolveComparisonSource`, `exportGitTree`, `resolveBuildRev`, `isSourceDirty` and `resolvePackageVersion` went through `SourceRepo`; the archive is read whole (about 0.9 MB for this overlay) and then extracted, so a git that fails leaves nothing and an extraction error is reported only when git succeeded, as before. `frontmatterName` is gone, `validateGaduFrontmatter` keeps its rules over `skills.ReadFrontmatter`. `cmd`: `runPipkgCore(source, ...)`, `runRuntimeCore(commands, source, ...)`, `newRuntimeRegistry(registries, commands, source, options)`, `newPipkgSource(os.Environ())`. The lint's `parseFrontmatterFields` is not touched: it knows the five required fields, the metadata block and the shapes a description must not take, and the lint rules are written against it.
  - **Behavior of git: identical, and two decisions stopped on.** The adapter takes the environment and the deadline as options, and `cmd` hands the two that held before: the environment of the process, whole, and no deadline. Both are in `pipkgGitOptions`, and a test says so. **Decision 1, the environment git sees (recommendation: scrub the variables that locate a repository).** Evidence in the comparison below: with `GIT_DIR` pointing at another repository in the environment, both binaries compare the package against that repository (`compared against main (c4667eb07131) ... skills/pi-skill/SKILL.md: changed`); a git hook that runs `pipkg check` sets exactly that. I recommend removing `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_NAMESPACE` and `GIT_PREFIX` and keeping everything else (`PATH`, `HOME`, `GIT_CONFIG_*`, the locale, the Windows variables git needs) rather than an allowlist, which breaks on a platform whose variables nobody listed; it is one function in `cmd` and the adapter does not change. It is a behavior change, so it waits for the owner. **Decision 2, a deadline on git (recommendation: two minutes a call).** Nothing stops a hung `git` today (a lock, a network file system), as nothing stopped `pi`. Every call the builder makes is local and takes milliseconds except `status --untracked-files=all` and `archive`, which take seconds on a large tree; two minutes is far above that and finite. It is `Options.Timeout` in `pipkgGitOptions`, zero today. It is a behavior change (a git that ran longer would be reported as a failure), so it waits for the owner too.
  - **Behavior of the frontmatter: four differences, all on input that was malformed or odd, classified.** The build and the Pi link now read with the lint's fence rules. (1) A SKILL.md or GADU.md that starts with a byte-order mark is accepted; it was refused as "no frontmatter block" / "missing opening --- delimiter". (2) A key that is indented under another key is no longer a top-level one: `metadata:\n  name: x` used to satisfy the build's name check, and `metadata:\n  description: x` the link's description check. (3) A SKILL.md whose block is never closed is refused (`frontmatter is not terminated`) even when a `name:` came first. (4) A fence that is indented is not a fence. Real files are unaffected (the real overlay and `pipkg-real-repo` below are byte-identical). The CHANGELOG says it.
  - **Smaller equivalent changes.** `pipkg build` now also reads `LABDRIAN_PI_DEPLOY_REF` (it builds the same `Packages` as the check; the build never used the ref). The unreachable `preparing git archive` error (a failing `StdoutPipe`) is gone; a git that cannot be started says `pipkg: starting git archive: ...` as before, a git that fails `pipkg: git archive <rev>: exit status N (<stderr>)`, the same words.
  - **Binary comparison.** Old binary (`2156d0e`) against the new (the tip of the code commits), fresh temp worlds at identical paths (`<W>`), `PATH=<W>/bin:/usr/bin:/bin` with a `pi` that only appends its arguments to a log (the real one in `~/.npm-global/bin` was never reachable), temp git repositories with fixed commit dates (so ids match), `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL/SYSTEM=/dev/null`, `HOME` and `STATE_DIR` in the world: 31 scenarios, 27 byte-identical in stdout, stderr, exit code, the `pi` log and the whole file tree. Identical: `pipkg build|check` on a plain directory, on a repository (clean, tagged `v1.4.2`, dirty build, an untracked file under `skills/`, stale after a commit, drift after an edit, with a `pi/` directory, on a branch that is not main with and without `LABDRIAN_PI_DEPLOY_REF`, with no git on the `PATH`, with a foreign `GIT_DIR`), refusals for a SKILL.md with no name, no frontmatter or another name, CRLF with a quoted name, the real repository (the worktree itself, read only: build then check, 32 files), and `runtime status|install|update|uninstall --target pi` and `--target all` with a fake `pi` that works, fails, is absent, with `LABDRIAN_PI_SKIP_SUBAGENTS=1`, with an ambiguous `tools`, with no description and no fence in GADU.md. The four that differ are the four above (SKILL.md with a BOM: refused then, built now; unterminated with a name: built then, refused now; a nested `name:`: built then, refused now; GADU.md with a BOM: refused then, linked now) and nothing else. The harness is `scratchpad/cmp/compare.py`.
  - **RED and mutation evidence.** `ReadFrontmatter`: tests against missing symbols, then the code; mutations: a nested key read as top level, an unindented list not counted, `Text()` not unquoting, the entry guard removed (a list item before any key panicked, so the test for it was added), each killed; `\r` trimming was dead code (`TrimSpace` handles it) and is deleted. `execrunner.Output`: streams merged, the process environment leaking in, the environment ignored, the deadline error not wrapped, `WaitDelay` removed (30.00s in the new grandchild test), each killed. `gitsource`: the fake runner pins the argv of all eight calls, the environment, and no deadline when `Timeout` is zero; mutations (environment dropped, timeout dropped or always on, directory ignored, a wrong flag, ancestor and descendant swapped, untracked files dropped, status not trimmed, the pattern or the rev ignored, the path of `HasPath` ignored, a deadline reported as "starting") each fail a test; against a real git in a temp repository, a `GIT_DIR` set in the process does not reach it. `pipkg`: the SKILL.md and GADU.md refusal tables passed on the old code first (characterization) and the four new cases failed (RED); mutations (the name not unquoted, the fences swapped, the key renamed, the tools list not counted, the description read unquoted) each fail a test. The port: with a fake `SourceRepo`, mutations on the paths of the dirty check, the version pattern, the `v` prefix, the error-as-clean rule, the `pi` path, the order of the refs and the override, the ancestry direction and the worktree basis each fail a test. `cmd`: a recording source proves `pipkg build` and `runtime install --target pi` ask git through the source they are given; replacing it with `NoRepository` in either place fails a test.
  - **Safety.** No test starts `pi`, `claude`, `codex` or `opencode`: the `TestMain` guards of `runtime` and `cmd` and their source scan are unchanged (the new tests there import no process adapter), and `ps` shows no `pi` or leftover `sleep`. Git runs only against temporary repositories, under `gitEnvironment` (`GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and `_SYSTEM` are `/dev/null`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, no `HOME`, no inherited variable but `PATH`); the `pipkg` fixtures' own `runGit` uses it too. No test or comparison touched `~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, `~/.pi`, `~/.config/opencode`, `~/.local/state/labdrian`, `~/.labdrian-overlay` or Engram; `HOME` was never reassigned globally (the comparison passes it to the child only). The real registry was only read.
  - **Real repository (read only).** `engine skills list` (37 lines, exit 0, empty stderr) and `skills validate --registry skills.registry.yaml --manifest overlay.manifest --source-root skills` from the worktree root: exit 0, empty stderr.
  - **Verification (from `engine/`).** `gofmt -l .`: empty. `go vet ./...`: clean. `staticcheck ./...`: clean. `GOPROXY=off go test -count=1 ./...`: ok, every package. `go test -race ./pipkg/... ./skills/... ./runtime/... ./cmd/... ./execrunner/... ./piguard/...`: ok. `GOOS=darwin go build ./...` and `GOOS=windows go build ./pipkg/... ./skills/... ./runtime/... ./execrunner/... ./piguard/...`: ok. `TestArchitectureFollowsTheDependencyRule` with `knownDebt = archguard.Debt{}`: ok.
  - **What is left.** `LABDRIAN_OVERLAY_DIR` in `runtime/opencode.go` and `GaduLinkStateForTest` (H26); `GENTLE_PI_AGENTS_CHILD` in `cmd/shaper.go` and the other `cmd` environment reads (H30/H31). `cmd` still reads `os.Environ()` and `os.Getenv` in `runPipkg`, `runRuntime` and `runPipkgCore`, which is the composition root; moving them into a `deps` struct is H31. The owner decided the environment git sees (decision 1, below); the deadline on git is decision 2 (option A, two minutes). The OpenCode abort decision is deferred to H26, where the OpenCode adapter is split.

  - **Review findings of batch 20 (12 in groups B, C and D; 11 resolved, none declined; all four groups approved).** `R1-001` (WARNING, `5ea3a2c`): `gitsource` refuses a ref that starts with a dash with `*InvalidRefError` naming it, before it runs git, and every ref reaches git after `--end-of-options` (`cat-file -e`, `merge-base --is-ancestor`, `rev-parse --verify`, `describe`, `archive`; for `archive` the paths follow the rev with no `--`, because `archive --end-of-options <rev> -- <paths>` is refused by git 2.43; the status paths keep their `--`). Git 2.24 (2019) is the first that knows `--end-of-options`; this machine has 2.43 and the CI image (`ubuntu-latest`) is newer, so the safest equivalent was not needed. Tests: a dash ref is refused for all seven ref-taking calls with the fake runner proving git was not run; the argv of each call has the terminator before the ref; a real git given `--output=<file>` as the ref writes no file. Mutations: each terminator removed, the refusal removed (also from `Export` alone), each fail a test. `R4-silent-timeout-degradation` (WARNING, `5ea3a2c`): `IsWorkTree`, `HasCommit`, `HasPath` and `IsAncestor` return `(bool, error)`. An exit status of git is `false, nil`; git not installed (`exec.ErrNotFound`) is `false, nil`, which keeps today's behavior (the comparison's `pipkg-no-git-on-path` scenario stays identical); a deadline, a signal (`kill -9` in the test) and a start failure other than not-found are errors carrying `Unavailable() bool`, and the builder surfaces them (`pipkg: asking git: ...` in the check, `pipkg: git could not answer: ...` from the status, the HEAD and the tag lookups in the build) where it used to build with no recorded commit or compare with the worktree. A failure of the value methods that is an answer (an unknown ref, no tag) is read as before. CHANGELOG line added. Mutations: not-found treated as unavailable, a signal treated as an answer, the predicate swallowing the error, the builder swallowing it, and the check falling back to the worktree on an error each fail a test. `R4-export-buffered` (`20a1bee`): `execrunner.Runner.WithMaxOutput(n)` bounds each stream `Output` holds; a program that prints more is stopped and `Output` returns `ErrOutputTooLarge`; `cmd` bounds git at 256 MiB (a hundred times the archive of this overlay). Mutations: either stream uncapped, an off-by-one at the bound, the error hidden, and the program not stopped (a shell that ignores SIGPIPE and sleeps: 30s) each fail. The wiring in `cmd` has no test of its own (the bound is a private field of the runner). `R2-frontmatter-dup-msg` and `R2-readfm-list-logic` (`50d3fbd`): `SplitSkillFile` takes the message from `FrontmatterFault.String()` and fails on any fault; the list branch of `ReadFrontmatter` is split from the indented-body branch and commented; a test pins the message; mutation: the literal back fails it. `R2-001`, `R2-002`, `R2-003`, `R2-compare-doc-name` and `R2-skillname-unreadable-ok` (`e56c733`): the provenance-root comment names the real argument and says the version is the newest `v*` tag reachable from the ref; the `exportGitTree` doc says the ref can come from the environment and is not trusted; `Stale` is documented as a proper ancestor; the `Compare` doc says Compare and how `Check` relates; `skillName` and `validateGaduFrontmatter` treat any `ReadFrontmatter` error as a missing fence. `R3-001` (`e56c733`): `TestRunPipkgCore_BuildIgnoresTheDeployRef` runs the build with `LABDRIAN_PI_DEPLOY_REF=--output=...` and a recording source and asserts exit 0 and that no ref was asked about; mutation: a `HasCommit` call in the build fails it. **Held for the owner:** `R4-git-no-deadline` and the question of which environment git sees (decisions 1 and 2 above); `pipkgGitOptions` is unchanged. **Carried to batch 21 under the owner's rule (group A, 4 suggestions, untouched):** `R1-001` (`stopRecordedProcess` kills a pid read from a file, which could be stale), `R2-001` (the `CheckTestSources` doc on constants inside functions), `R2-002` (`time.Since` called twice in the execrunner bound check) and `R4-001` (the bound is evaluated after `Run` returns and the sleep is killed only at cleanup), all in `execrunner/execrunner_test.go` and `piguard/sources.go`; the tests added in this batch for `Output` repeat the `time.Since` pattern and wait for the same fix.
  - **Review of the fixes (group E, `88cd331..4d19233`): 3 warnings and 1 suggestion; the warnings and one defect are fixed, the suggestion is carried.** `R2-surfaced-nil-on-error` (WARNING, `9fadd93`): `surfaced`, which returned nil for a failure, is gone; `resolveBuildRev`, `isSourceDirty` and `resolvePackageVersion` ask `couldNotAnswer` first and return the zero value with `unanswered(err)` for a git that could not answer, and take the fallback (no commit, a clean tree, `developmentVersion`) only on the answer "there is none". `TestProvenanceNeverReturnsAValueWithAnError` covers both paths of each; mutations (the development version returned with the error, a stop swallowed in the rev or in the status, the fallback removed) each fail. `R3-001` (WARNING, `7bdcc44`): `cappedBuffer` accepts the part of a write that fits and refuses the rest, so a failed `Output` holds a prefix exactly the bound long (documented); the tests assert the bytes of a cut inside a ten-byte write (1005); mutations (the prefix not kept, one byte short) fail them. `R3-002` (WARNING, `7bdcc44`): `gitsource` is tested with the real process adapter under a bound of 100 bytes: `Export`, `Resolve` and `HasCommit` report `ErrOutputTooLarge` in the chain and marked unavailable (for `HasCommit` an error, not a no); `Runner.MaxOutput()` reads the bound (the test that `pipkgGitRunner()` in `cmd` carries `pipkgGitMaxOutput` was claimed here but missing; it was added in `f3bf55e`, see group F); mutations (the classification returning the raw error, the bound dropped from the runner, the accessor returning zero) fail. **Orphaned `sleep 30` (defect, `c655ed3`):** it was not the `execrunner` tests. Running each package alone and looking at `ps` after it found `engine/shelltest`: the wedged-engine case of `overlay_longterm_mem_test.sh` wrote a bash stub that ran `sleep 30` as a child, and the test killed the bash pid with `kill -9`, leaving the sleep (ppid 1) alive for its thirty seconds. The stub now `exec`s the sleep, the case fails when the stub has a child (`pgrep -P`) and waits for the pid to be gone; with `exec` removed the harness fails. After `go test -count=3 ./execrunner/...`, after `./shelltest` and after the full `GOPROXY=off go test -count=1 ./...`, `ps` shows no `sleep`. The `execrunner` grandchild tests were left as they are (their cleanup is the carried group A item). **Carried to batch 21:** `R2-cappedbuffer-short-write` (SUGGESTION, `execrunner.go:52-60`; the exact cut above resolves its content as a side effect: the bound is now an exact cut and the overflowing write's fitting part is kept and documented, and the test asserts equality; it stays listed for the reviewer to close), together with the four of group A.
  - **Owner decision 2 of batch 20 (2026-10-09): option A, a deadline of two minutes per git call.** `pipkgGitTimeout` in `cmd/pipkg_source.go` sets `gitsource.Options.Timeout`; a call that runs past it is a git that could not answer (the `R4-silent-timeout-degradation` fix), never a no. RED: `TestGitRunsUnderTheDecidedDeadline` failed with `0s, want 2m0s` before the constant. CHANGELOG line.
  - **Review of group H (`0af48fb..067e9a2`): one warning, fixed; one suggestion, carried.** `R3-001` (WARNING): the CHANGELOG entry of the frontmatter reader still said git ran "with the environment of the process and no deadline, as before"; it now points at the two entries of decisions 1 and 2. `R3-002` (`cmd/pipkg_source_test.go`, a `cmd`-level test that the deadline reaches the runner and stops a hung git) is carried to batch 21 with the other six.
  - **Owner decision 1 of batch 20 (2026-10-09): option A, the git of the package builder does not inherit the repository-locating variables.** `pipkgGitOptions` in `cmd/pipkg_source.go` removes `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_NAMESPACE` and `GIT_PREFIX` by name from the environment of the process and keeps the rest in order (`PATH`, `HOME`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and the others pass); the adapter is unchanged. An empty environment stays an empty slice, because the adapter reads nil as "the environment of the process". Tests (RED before, three failing): the scrubbed list is the decided one, a variable named like a banned one is kept, the rest passes in order, the filter is by name with `=` in values. Mutations (a name dropped from the list, the filter inverted, the environment not filtered, a nil result for an empty one) each fail a test. **Binary comparison:** 26 of 31 scenarios identical, 5 differ: the four frontmatter cases above and one new, intended difference, `pipkg-git-dir-env-leak` (a foreign `GIT_DIR` set in the environment): the old binary compared against the worktree and said the overlay was not a repository (a `GIT_DIR` naming nothing), or against the other repository and reported drift (a `GIT_DIR` naming another repository); the new binary compares against the overlay's own repository, `compared against main (4b245742b049)`, and finds no drift, with or without `GIT_WORK_TREE`. CHANGELOG line added. **Decision 2 (a deadline on git) is still held;** `pipkgGitOptions` keeps `Timeout` at zero and a test says so.

- **Phase D batch 21 (H26: `runtime/core`, `runtime/opencodeprompt`, the split of `pi.go` and `opencode.go`; the seven carried suggestions; the owner's OpenCode decision).** Branch `refactor/phase9-b21` from `df413f7`; fourteen code commits of the rewritten history (below) and seven of the fix round, plus this entry. `engine/runtime` is no longer one package that holds everything. `runtime/core` (domain) is what needs no machine: `Target` and its constants, `CapabilityStatus`, `Action`, `LifecycleResult`, the `Adapter` port, the `Registry` with its `Factory`, `Config`, and the prompt rules (`MutatePrompt`, `InjectPrompt`, `StripPrompt` and the three `HasExact`/`Canonical` helpers); it imports `capability`, `contract` and the pure standard library. `runtime/opencodeprompt` (domain) derives, verifies and hashes the prompt config of the OpenCode plugin from the text of the contracts a `ContractSource` hands it. `runtime` (adapter) keeps the adapters (Claude, Codex, OpenCode, Pi, longterm-mem) and the `Register*` functions, and reads no environment: the allowlist of the environment scan is deleted, so the scan has no exception left. `knownDebt` stays empty; two rings added (`runtime/core`, `runtime/opencodeprompt`, both domain).

H26: `9ce55e3` add `runtime/core` (+999 -3 / 11), `bedd67f` add `runtime/opencodeprompt` (+949 / 9). The switch to the core was one commit (`50ebb3b`, 30 files) that the native review refused with `lens_context_budget_exceeded`, so the branch was rewritten from `bedd67f` into nine commits, each compiling and each green under `GOPROXY=off go test -count=1 ./...`: `3c58101` the old vocabulary, registry and `Config` become aliases of the core (+43 -284 / 3), then one family each, `4675528` Claude (+75 -71 / 4), `09a8844` Codex (+53 -50 / 2), `42c655d` longterm-mem and `Config.DefaultClaudeMCPConfig` (+109 -96 / 7), `a690b35` OpenCode (+75 -73 / 2), `6ea2c52` Pi (+81 -78 / 3), `2dc1786` `cmd` (+29 -27 / 3), and two deletions, `5944f2f` the old tests (+31 -582 / 4) and `276d3b9` the forwards and the old files (+45 -142 / 5). All approved and closed.
  - **The seven carried suggestions, none declined.** `R1-001` and `R4-001` (`dac28f3`): the grandchild the fake programs leave holding the pipes is started as `/bin/sh -c 'echo $$ > "$1"; exec /bin/sleep 29.5'`, so it writes its own id before it becomes the sleep and a program stopped early leaves neither a sleep nor an id; `stopRecordedProcess` kills an id only while `ps -p <id> -o args=` still reads `/bin/sleep 29.5` (a reused id is left alone, and so is one whose command line cannot be read). The tests pin both branches (`TestStopRecordedProcessLeavesAnotherProcessAlone`, which first proves the lister works so a pass is not vacuous, and `TestStopRecordedProcessKillsTheGrandchildItRecorded`); the lister is named by path, `/bin/ps`, because the PATH of that package's tests is empty by design. Mutations: the identity check removed (killed, and my first version of the test survived it because a killed child that is not yet waited for still answers signal 0, so the test reads the command line, which shows `[sleep] <defunct>`), the kill removed (killed). The loop-back of the pid after `exec` is by construction and has no mutation test. `R2-002` (`dac28f3`): every bound check reads `time.Since` once (`elapsed, bound := ...`). `R2-cappedbuffer-short-write` (`dac28f3`): the substance was done by the exact cut of `7bdcc44` and is pinned by `TestOutputStopsAProgramThatPrintsMoreThanTheBound` and `TestOutputBoundsStandardErrorToo` (1005 bytes held, not a multiple of the ten a write carries); verified load-bearing by letting `Write` keep the whole write (both fail, 1010 and 1020 bytes); the missing part was the doc of `Write`, added (it says what a short write returns and why). `R2-001` (`9c06776`): the `CheckTestSources` doc says a constant declared inside a function is not read and that a name is looked up in the constants of the whole package whatever the scope, so a function-level constant that shadows a package-level one is read as the package one, with a false positive and a false negative; `TestCheckTestSourcesReadsAShadowedConstantAsThePackageOne` pins both; mutation: the package constants not read (killed). `R3-runner-maxoutput-nonzero` (`f005ef9`): the bound test fails first when `MaxOutput() <= 0`; mutation: `pipkgGitMaxOutput = 0` (killed). `R3-002` of group H (`f005ef9`): `newPipkgSource` is now `newPipkgSourceWithin(environ, pipkgGitTimeout)`, the one place the runner and the options meet, and `pipkgGitOptions` takes the deadline as a parameter, so no package variable is touched; `TestAHungGitIsStoppedByTheDeadlineThePackageBuilderHandsIt` puts a `git` that sleeps thirty seconds on a PATH of its own, hands a 200 ms deadline, and asks `IsWorkTree`: it gets an error that says git could not answer, in well under ten seconds, and not a `true`; mutation: the deadline replaced by zero (killed after the full thirty seconds, with `IsWorkTree = true`), and `TestGitRunsUnderTheDecidedDeadline` now pins `pipkgGitTimeout == 2m`.
  - **What moved where.** `runtime.go` became `core/lifecycle.go` (vocabulary and port) and `core/prompt.go` (the prompt rules); `config.go` became `core/config.go`; `registry.go` split into `core/registry.go` (`Registry`, `Factory`) and `register.go` (`RegisterClaude`, `RegisterCodex`, `RegisterOpenCode`, `RegisterPi`); their tests moved with them (`core/lifecycle_test.go`, `prompt_test.go`, `config_test.go`, `registry_test.go`, and `target_vocabulary_test.go`, the scan that every `Target` constant is a name of the capability vocabulary, which reads the files that now hold the constants). The one constant outside `core`, the `longterm-mem` label, is not a runtime and is not scanned, as before. `claudeMCPConfigPath` (the `~/.claude.json` rule) became `Config.DefaultClaudeMCPConfig`, next to the other defaults, with a test (RED: `undefined`). `opencode.go` (652 lines) became `opencode.go` (the lifecycle), `opencode_records.go` (the config record and the active marker), `opencode_contracts.go` (the `ContractSource` over the overlay on disk and the embedded guard, and the lookup of the checkout) and `opencode_plugin.go` (the embedded plugin, its version and hash); the JSON codecs, the verification and the hash of the prompt config went to `opencodeprompt` (`config.go`, `derive.go`, `verify.go`, `source.go`). `pi.go` (579 lines) became `pi.go` (the adapter and its lifecycle), `pi_subagents.go` (the Subagents runner) and `pi_gadu.go` (the GADU.md link); `isPiMcpRegistered` moved next to the other reads of Pi's files in `pi_settings.go`. `LABDRIAN_OVERLAY_DIR` is read once, by `cmd` (`runtimeConfigFromEnv`), into `Config.LabdrianOverlayDir`, handed to the adapter as `OpenCodeOptions.OverlayDir` by `RegisterOpenCode`; the adapter judges it as before (blank is none, relative is refused naming the variable) and, when none is given, still walks up from the working directory, which is a file-system lookup and not an environment read. `GaduLinkStateForTest`, a production export only a test used, is deleted: `TestGaduLinkState_Matrix` is now an internal test of `gaduLinkState` over a scratch directory (it no longer builds a whole Pi package to test three file-system calls), and a case for a target spelled with a redundant element (`Clean`) was added. The one mutant of `gaduLinkState` that survives is the explicit symlink-mode check, which is equivalent: `Readlink` on a regular file fails and ends in the same `conflict`. The Pi ports (`CommandRunner`, `PackageBuilder`, `PiPorts`) stay in `runtime` with the adapter, as H24 decided: nothing in the inner ring uses them, so a `core` that owned them would own a port with no client of its own.
  - **Owner decision of 2026-10-09 on the OpenCode loader: option A, a malformed contract aborts, primary or secondary (`af3837a`).** Until then the loader aborted on a malformed primary contract and dropped a malformed secondary one (`oo-quality-contract.md`) in silence, so a plugin could ship without its guard and nothing said why (`R3-opencode-secondary-contract-silent-drop`, deferred to H26 since batch 8). In the move (`bedd67f`, `ecc3259`) the behavior was kept exactly and isolated in one function, `onMalformed`, with tests pinning both branches through `Derive` and directly. The decision is a change to that function: it now returns a `*MalformedContractError{Path, Err}` for every contract (the review then found that nothing was left for the function to decide, and `bb8c08f` removed it: the two places that parse a contract return the error, and the decision is recorded on the type), `skills/_shared/oo-quality-contract.md: <parse error>`, wrapping the parse error (`errors.As` reaches the `contract` error behind it), and the install or update exits 1, `partial`, writing nothing.   - **Every text the loop parsed, and what it is now.** (1) stderr trimmed equal to `error: registry exists but is empty — refusing to propagate over it` with exit 1: now `Outcome Empty`. (2) stdout containing `project does not use the overlay (no-op)` with no exit and no write: now `Outcome Absent`. (3) `wrote`, a flag the loop set from inside the write function it injected: now `Outcome Written` with the bytes in `Attempt.Written`. (4) the exit code of the core (any hard failure forwarded at once): now a returned error, one of eight types, each rendered by `propagateMessage`. Against the new code, `c0d83cf` passes all 33 byte for byte; the one change is in the older contract goldens (below). A mutation proof that they bite: the attempt bound 3 to 2 fails 17 cases, each of the two evidence terms dropped that the goldens can reach fails one.

Every other contract golden is the same bytes. (b) Nothing else in the program: the old binary (built from `ea2dafd`) and the new one were run over 31 scenarios in temp worlds (a fixed neutral root, `env -i`, a child-only `HOME`, a minimal `PATH`): written, unchanged, embedded contracts with and without an explicit path, absent, absent and required, empty, white space, a contract with no frontmatter, a malformed list, missing, unreadable, an unknown embedded contract, no arguments, no contract source, no registry flag, an unreadable registry, a registry that is a directory, a path through a plain file, a write that fails after the lock was taken and the run again, `..` segments, a symlinked registry, the last `--registry` winning, a flag with no value at the end, a 0600 registry, the lock held for 6 s (error naming the lock after 2.06 s old, 2.06 s new), held for 1 s (0.66 s and 0.64 s, written), held while the arguments are refused, and twelve concurrent runs of two contracts. The settingsfile, runtime and execrunner mutations are in each commit's tests: `missing` without each of its five kinds (5 killed), `sameFailure` back to the reason only (killed) and without its separator boundary (killed after a new case), the manifest without each of its four arms (4 killed), the recorder without its arguments, the lister's exit 1 taken as an error, a message without the pid, the replay building its hook command on the world (330 failures).

(2) The adapter keeps the temp-file-and-rename of the old `atomicWriteFile` and does not move to `atomicfile`: the two differ in the temporary file name, the words of the errors (`atomicfile: create temporary file: ...`) and whether the directory is synced, which the goldens and the owner's identical-behavior rule do not allow to change here; the unification is a separate decision for the owner. (3) The lock stays in `cmd`: its key comes from the raw argument vector before the arguments are judged (a held lock is reported before an argument error, and a flag eaten as a value still names a lock), a quirk the binary comparison pins and H31 may revisit.

Two quirks of the unchanged program are pinned and not fixed: a loose ref that holds no object name hides a valid packed ref (`lookupRef` answers the loose one), and `hookwire` drops a relative `cwd` of the hook input, so the process directory is used. The rule `sha256(Clean(common))` is `projection.RepoKeyOf`; the symbolic links are still resolved by the adapter before it. `runWorkflowBind`, `runWorkflowUnbind` and `runWorkflowBinding` keep the arguments, the exit codes and the JSON, and ask `BindWorkflow.Bind`, `Unbind` and `Describe`; the stale-binding judgment, the compare-and-replace, the read-back and the idempotence are the use case's. `userPromptSubmit` and `preToolUse` keep the decoding and the answer and ask `HookService.OnPrompt` and `OnToolCall`; the relevance of a tool, the directory fallback, the reading of the binding and the workflow, and the removal of the binding of a closed workflow are the service's. The words of the refusals are `bindingRefusal` (a type switch over the typed errors, pinned word for word in `TestBindingRefusalSaysEachRefusalInTheWordsOfTheVerbs`); `loadWorkflow` is `workflowReader`, the one place a store that cannot be built becomes an unavailable workflow. `verifyStored` is gone: its check is `ChangedConcurrentlyError`.
  - **The pointer shared with gitprov.** The two readers are not the same reader: `gitprov` insists on an exact one-line `gitdir: <path>` in a regular file and checks that the directory leads back to the worktree, because it verifies; `gitfs` strips the whitespace around the line and the path and follows a symbolic `.git`, because it finds and fails soft, and the key of every existing binding depends on it. What they share is the rule of what a line names (the text after a prefix, not empty, a relative path taken relative to the file's directory): `gitprov.PointerTarget`, used by `readPointer` and by `gitfs`. Making the two one reader would change a behavior of one of them, which is the owner's decision; flagged, not done. A third copy of the same reading is in `skills/projectidentity` (`commonDirOfPointer`), outside H29's list and not touched.

Exit codes, stdout and stderr are identical after the world's path, the times and the digests are written as placeholders (703 lines of transcript each; zero differences). There is no difference to classify.

(b) Test seams: `beforeStaleReplace`, `beforeHookUnbind`, `onGateStoreAccess` and `beforeGateDecision` are still package variables of `cmd`, now read in one place each (the decorators `seamedBindings` and `newGateHook` in `cmd/binding_wiring.go`); the tests that set them are unchanged. Removing them is H31.
  - **Decisions.** (1) The plan's row says `projection.HookService`; the application ring is a package of its own, so it is `projection/app.HookService`, as `propagator/app` was for H28. (2) The binding store is opened on first use (`lazyBindings`), so a command that never goes to the store (a workflow refused first, a tool the gate does not check) never builds it, as before, and a store that cannot be built answers every operation with the same words whichever operation came first; this is what let the service take a `projection.BindingStore` and not an opener. (3) `Project` and `Gate` are fields of `HookService` (the domain's functions when nil), so that the gate's `beforeGateDecision` is a wrapper around the policy and not a hook inside the service; the recovery of a panic stays in `cmd`, and a test pins that the service lets it through. (4) The clock is a port the use case owns (`app.Clock`); `cmd` hands it `time.Now`. (5) The pointer reading is shared as described above, not unified.

(c) The static test of `runtime_probe.go` allowed `os.UserHomeDir` and `os.Getenv`; it now allows no `os` at all.
  - **Decisions.** (1) Two values and not one: `process` is what a core takes as plain parameters (so a test captures them), `deps` is what the verbs are built over; the cores keep their explicit streams so the hundreds of test calls changed by one leading argument and not by a rewrite. (2) `getwd` is in `deps`, not in `process`: the skills verbs need it beyond the wrappers. (3) `probeTimeout` stays a field and the default a constant; `workflowProber` in the program is a closure over `deps` that reads the home and PATH when a verb asks, as before. (4) The seams are decorators built by the test over the store and the gate it is handed; the store of a run is still opened lazily, so a decorator runs only when the store opened (the gate test that counts accesses has a working store). (5) A package-level table that nothing assigns (`skillsCLIVerbs`, the flag specs, `repositoryLocatingVariables`) stays; the guard makes it read-only by test and not by type.

(2) `workflow.gentleReviewProfiles` and `profileReliesOnGentleReview`: gone; `observationsFor` reads `profile.MemoryDefault` and `profile.ReliesOnGentleReview` of the profile the catalog returned (before, it asked `memoryscope` for the default of the built-in profile of the same name, so an injected profile's own data was ignored). (3) `workflowprofile.requiredStages(name)` and `requiredChecks(name)`: gone, with the table they repeated; `catalog` declares each profile once and `Validate` compares with it (one optional check, marked, for `maintenance`). (4) `Validate`'s `profile.Name == "sdd"` rule that verify precedes archive: removed, because it could not fail (the stage sequence is already required to equal the catalog's, in which verify precedes archive); `stageBefore` went with it. (5) The prefix rule written twice (`stageOrderPrefix` in `lifecycle.go` for `Verify`, and the loop in `projection.nextStage`) and the next-stage index in `RecordStage`: `WorkflowProfile.CheckStagePrefix` (the same messages, byte for byte) and `NextStage`. `RecordStage` still does not check the prefix, as before. Reported, not moved, on purpose: the catalog lookups by name (`workflowprofile.Resolve(name)` in `workflow.go` for an event's profile, in `projection` and `cmd/memory.go`, and the lifecycle's `ProfileCatalog`): the name is the profile's identity in the log, and H33 replaces it with a snapshot. Dead and removed, proved with `rg` over the whole tree before and the compiler after: `ResolveForCompatibility`, `ValidateResume`, `CompatibilityMode` and its constants, `ResumeState`, `ErrUnsupportedCombination`, `ErrInvalidResumeState` (only the package and its own tests used them).
  - **What moved where.** `workflowprofile`: types `MemoryScope`, `MemorySource`, `MemoryDefault`; fields `MemoryDefault` and `ReliesOnGentleReview` of `WorkflowProfile`, both `json:"-"` so a profile still serializes exactly its seven contract fields (the existing test pins it); the explanatory comments of each default moved from `memoryscope` to the profile that owns it. `memoryscope.DefaultFor(workflowprofile.WorkflowProfile)`. `workflow.DefaultDependencyProbeTimeout` (was unexported). `deps.probeBound()`. `cmd/memory.go` and `projection.resolvePlan` resolve the profile by name, then ask `DefaultFor`.
  - **Binary comparison.** `engine-old` (the tree at the first commit, behavior of `96ed12d`) and `engine-new` (the tip) over every catalog profile, with nothing present and with everything present (10 cases), in throwaway worlds (HOME, state, PATH and working directory under a temporary root; the script `compare_profiles.py` is outside the repository): `memory plan` with and without a Goal, `workflow create`, `start`, a stage out of order, an unknown stage, every stage, one stage too many, `status`, `pause`, `resume`, `verify`, `close` (17 to 19 invocations each); exit code and both streams compared, and the whole workflow log compared as bytes after masking the `at` timestamp and the two digests that chain over it. **Old against new: no difference** (log sizes equal in every case, 2,180 to 9,990 bytes).
  - **Differences, all classified.** (a) None in any verb, exit code or log byte. (b) For a caller that builds a lifecycle over its own catalog, the dependencies recorded follow the profile that catalog returns and not the built-in one of the same name; the program's catalog is the built-in one, so nothing differs in it. (c) `Validate` no longer carries the unreachable verify-before-archive rule. (d) `workflowprofile` no longer exports the resume and compatibility API.

No survivor.

  - **Review groups proposed (contiguous, each under 2,000 lines and 15 files, but for the data).** A `5743c4d..076e484` (2 files, ~30 lines); B `59876df..90f8880` (15 files, the ten logs and five transcripts: read as data, recorded from the unchanged program); C `b10ec0e` (3 files, ~350 lines: the harness that replays them, and the hook case); D `83c8e53..d916079` (9 files, ~1,150 lines: the snapshot and the event); E `84082f6..20e5a27` (9 files, ~850 lines: the lifecycle, the projection and the drift finding); F `c0d2844..7be4876` (4 files, ~125 lines). The docs commit passive.
  - **The three carried suggestions, none declined.** `R2-001` (`gitfs/imports_test.go:136`, `sort.Strings`; carried a second time because the import block is not in the diff): `sort` is imported at `imports_test.go:10` (`rg -n '"sort"' engine/gitfs/imports_test.go`), the file builds and its tests pass; there is nothing to change in the code. The mutant that deletes the import fails `go vet ./gitfs` with `undefined: sort` at the first use, so the import is load-bearing and checked by the compiler; no commit, the evidence is here. `R2-001` (`cmd/profile_golden_test.go:63-73`, `5743c4d`): the premise that the prober assignment is loop-invariant is wrong: `hookWorld.env()` resets `w.deps` to the prober that confirms nothing on every call (`hook_golden_test.go:90`), so the assignment must follow each `env()`, and it is the world's `deps` the hooks run over (`runProjectionCore(w.deps, ...)`), not only the environment's. What the suggestion was after, that the loop mutate shared state inline and obscure that only the prober differs, is fixed: `hookWorld.envWithEverythingPresent()` is `env()` plus the prober, named and documented, and the case reads `e := w.envWithEverythingPresent()`. Mutants killed (the golden `prompt-projects-every-profile-with-its-dependencies-present` fails): the prober not set; `e.deps = w.deps` dropped. `R2-002` (`workflowprofile.go:268-276`, `076e484`): `equalSources` is deleted and `Validate` compares with `slices.Equal` (the module is Go 1.21, which has it); mutant (the sources compared sliced to length 0) killed by `TestValidateRequiresTheCatalogsTypedData` (the wider-scope and source-dropped rows).
  - **Q6, taken literally.** The workflow's created event records a snapshot of the profile as the catalog gave it, and the workflow is self-contained from then on; the catalog only serves a new workflow (`Create`) and a request (`memory plan --profile`).
  - **The v2 event, as designed (written down here after the tests that pin it, which were written before the code).** `WorkflowEvent.Version` is 1 or 2 (`EventVersionNameOnly`, `EventVersionSnapshot`; `EventVersion` is the one written for a new workflow, 2). A log has the version of its created event from its first line to its last: `State.Version` is set by the created event, `CheckTransition` refuses an event of another version with `ErrMixedVersions`, so `ClassifyLog` classifies such a log as drifted and `AdmitAppend` refuses to extend it, and the lifecycle appends events of the log's version (`eventWith`: the version of the replayed state, the current one for a log that does not exist). So a v1 log is never rewritten or mixed: it stays v1 to its close. The version 2 created event adds two fields, written after `profile` and before `role_chain_id`: `"profile_snapshot"` (an object, `omitempty` as a pointer) and `"profile_digest"` (a string, `omitempty`); a version 1 event omits both, so its bytes are as they were. `profile_snapshot` is `workflowprofile.Snapshot`: `{"name","stages":[{"name","depends_on":[...]}],"roles","checks","memory_policy","review_policy","delivery_policy","memory_default":{"scope","sources":[...]},"relies_on_gentle_review"}`, all of what `Validate`, `RecordStage`, `Verify`, `observationsFor` (the dependencies recorded) and the projection read of a profile (the seven wire fields and the typed data of H32). Lists are never null (an empty list is `[]`; `Snapshot.Validate` refuses a null one, so one profile has one encoding). `profile_digest` is the SHA-256, lowercase hex, of `json.Marshal(snapshot)`: the compact encoding of the struct with its fields in declaration order and `encoding/json`'s escaping, the same encoding rule `EventDigest` uses for the event. Validation of a version 2 created event, with no look at the catalog: `profile` a safe identifier; the snapshot present, of the profile the event names, well formed (`Snapshot.Validate`: the seven fields populated, stages named once and depending only on earlier ones, lists present, a known memory scope and known sources) and within bounds (at most `MaxStages` stages, names within `MaxStageLength`, at most `MaxProfileListLength` (64) roles and checks, every text within `MaxProfileTextLength` (4096 runes)); and the digest equal to the snapshot's. A version 2 verified event's `checked.profile` is a safe name, and `CheckTransition` requires it to be the workflow's profile (version 1 never compared them and still does not). A version 1 created event must hold neither field and still names a catalog profile. `ParseWorkflowEvent` checks unknown keys inside the snapshot, each stage and the memory default, exact case. The pins: `TestAVersion2CreatedEventIsEncodedAsRecorded` (the line written out by hand, its event digest the SHA-256 of those bytes), `TestASnapshotIsEncodedAndDigestedAsRecordedInTheLog` (the encoding by hand, its digest computed outside the program with `printf | sha256sum`), `TestAVersion1EventIsEncodedWithoutTheSnapshotFields`.
  - **Every consumer of a profile identified by name, and where each went.** `rg` over the non-test code finds these name lookups now: `workflow.ProfileOf(state, catalog)` (the snapshot if the log has one, else `catalog.Resolve(state.Profile)`), used by `Lifecycle` (Start, Pause, Resume, Close through `stateEvent`; RecordStage; Verify) and by `projection` (`profileOf`: `nextStage`, `resolvePlan` for the context and the memory gate); `Lifecycle.Create` (the catalog serves the new workflow); `cmd/memory.go` (`memory plan --profile`, a request); `workflow.go` (the version 1 validation of a created and a verified event); `DetectProfileDrift` (the comparison). The reads of the name for display (`projection/gate.go`, the status JSON in `cmd/workflow.go`) are unchanged. The consumer the plan did not name and that mattered: the projection (the prompt hook's next stage and memory plan, and the PreToolUse memory gate). Left on the catalog, a drifted version 2 workflow would have been advanced by the lifecycle from its snapshot while the hooks told the agent the catalog's next stage and enforced the catalog's memory ceiling.
  - **What moved where.** `workflowprofile/snapshot.go` (new): `Snapshot`, `WorkflowProfile.Snapshot()`, `Snapshot.Profile()` (a conversion, so a field added to one type and not the other does not build), `Digest`, `Validate`, `DifferingFields`; `checkShape` extracted from `Validate` (same messages, same order); `MemoryDefault` got json tags (the profile still serializes its seven fields: `MemoryDefault` on the profile stays `json:"-"`). `workflow`: `EventVersionNameOnly/Snapshot`, `MaxProfileTextLength/ListLength`, the two event fields, `validateCreatedProfile`, `validateSnapshotBounds`, `validateCheckedProfile`, `checkNestedSnapshotFields`; `State.Version`, `State.ProfileSnapshot`, `ErrMixedVersions`; `ProfileOf`, `profileOf`, `stateEvent`; `drift.go`: `ProfileDrift`, `ProfileDriftKind` (`changed`, `retired`), `DetectProfileDrift`, `Lifecycle.ProfileDrift`; the `ProfileCatalog` port says an unknown profile wraps `workflowprofile.ErrUnknownProfile`. `projection/context.go`: `profileOf(state)`; `nextStage`, `resolvePlan`, `memoryPlanLines` take the state. The log adapter (`workflow/filelog`) did not change: it only reads and writes the lines the domain classifies and admits.
  - **Pins first.** `59876df`, `90f8880` and `b10ec0e`: ten v1 logs written by the unchanged program (the program built at `582896b`; five profiles, each created-only and running with two stages; one world each under a throwaway root; the script is outside the repository, as `compare_profiles.py` was) and `TestWorkflowV1Golden`: for each profile, the log of the running workflow continued through the other stages (the first again, an undeclared one and the last refused), a verify, a completed close and the status, and the log of the created workflow through a refused stage, a refused pause, a start, a second start refused, a pause, a refused stage, a resume, a stage, a verify and an abandoned close; every exit code, both streams, and the log line by line as version, seq, kind and the SHA-256 of the line with the timestamp and the chain digests masked (the lines are about a kilobyte each). Plus the hook case `prompt-and-gate-follow-a-version-1-log-of-every-profile`. They pass unchanged after every later commit, as do the profile and hook goldens of H32.
  - **Binary comparison.** `engine-old` (`582896b`) and `engine-new` (the tip) in throwaway worlds (HOME, state, PATH and working directory under one temporary root): for each of the five profiles, a fresh workflow through its whole life, a v1 log written by the old binary (running with two stages) continued by both, and a v1 log (created only) taken through pause, resume and an abandoned close by both; 205 pairs of invocations. **Old against new: no difference** in any exit code, stream or log event. The logs of the fresh workflows equal each other once `version` is set to 1, the two snapshot fields removed and the timestamps and chain digests masked; the new log is version 2 with the snapshot on the created event alone; the new binary never wrote a version 2 event into a v1 log. The control (the old binary run as the new) is flagged for the five fresh workflows, so the comparison can fail.
  - **Differences, all classified.** (a) Fresh workflows: the log of a new workflow is version 2 and its created event holds `profile_snapshot` and `profile_digest`: the recorded, deliberate format change (CHANGELOG). Nothing printed differs. (b) A version 1 log: none. (c) Downgrade: the old binary reads a version 2 log as `classification: foreign` (exit 0, an empty state) and refuses to append to it; a workflow created by this version is not to be carried on by an older program (CHANGELOG says so). (d) For a caller that builds a lifecycle over its own catalog, a new workflow's snapshot is what that catalog returns, and the dependencies it records follow it (unchanged since H32). (e) `Create` with a catalog that returns a profile of another name than asked is refused by the created event's validation (before, it recorded the requested name).
  - **RED and mutation evidence.** RED by build failure (the symbol did not exist): the whole of `snapshot_test.go`, `event_v2_test.go` and `drift_test.go`; RED by failure on the old code: seven lifecycle tests (`TestCreateRecordsTheSnapshotOfTheProfileTheCatalogGave`, `...WhateverItDeclares`, `TestCreateRefusesACatalogThatGivesAProfileOfAnotherName`, `TestCreateAppendsNothingWhenTheProfileTheCatalogGaveIsMalformed`, `TestAVersion2WorkflowGoesOnFromItsSnapshotWhateverTheCatalogSays`, `...ObservesTheDependenciesOfItsSnapshot`, `TestAnAbandonedCloseOfAVersion2Workflow...`) and three projection tests. The tests that pin behavior that must not change (version 1) pass on the old code by design. Four lifecycle tests that built a workflow with `Create` and then changed the catalog (`TestLifecycleAbandonSucceedsWhenProfileNoLongerResolves`, `...VerifyFailsWhenProfileNoLongerResolves`, `...VerifyFailsWhenStageOrderInvalid`, `TestLifecycleRecordStageFollowsTheResolversProfile`) tested version 1 behavior through a path that now writes version 2; they build a version 1 workflow by hand (`createVersion1Workflow`) and assert what they asserted. Mutants killed: the snapshot's `relies_on_gentle_review` or any list left out of the encoding or the diff; `Roles` or `DependsOn` aliased instead of copied; the null-list, scope, source, name and shape checks of `Snapshot.Validate` removed; the version check; the version 1 refusal of the snapshot fields; the name, digest, validation and each bound of a version 2 created event; the identifier check of the profile and of `checked.profile`; the created-only rule for both fields; the nested unknown-key checks (snapshot, stage, memory default); `ErrMixedVersions`; the verified-profile rule; `State.Version` and `State.ProfileSnapshot` not set; `ProfileOf` not preferring the snapshot, resolving by name, or version from the wrong place; the snapshot or its digest not recorded; `RecordStage`, `Verify` and `stateEvent` resolving by name; in the projection, the profile resolved by name, the state's goal dropped, the state passed to `nextStage`, `memoryPlanLines` or the gate stripped of its snapshot; in the drift finding, the version 1 exemption, the unknown-profile test, the digest comparison, the fields, the digests and `loadOwned`; through the built program, a created event written as version 1 or without the snapshot. Two guards survived and were removed as redundant, with the proof: the 64-hex test of `profile_digest` (the digest is compared with the one computed, which is always 64 lowercase hex) and the `profile.Name != profileName` check in `Create` (the created event's own validation refuses it).
  - **Decision stopped on: how drift is surfaced to the user (a product decision).** The finding exists, is typed and is tested (`workflow.ProfileDrift{Kind changed|retired, Profile, RecordedDigest, CurrentDigest, Fields}`, `workflow.DetectProfileDrift(state, catalog)`, `Lifecycle.ProfileDrift(project, workflow)`), and changes the behavior of no verb. No command prints it yet, because the choice among a warning in `status`, a refusal, or a new verb is the owner's. Recommendation: an additive field on `workflow status` (`"profile_drift": {"kind", "profile", "fields"}`, omitted when there is none, so every version 1 status and every undrifted version 2 status keeps its bytes) and one warning line on stderr of `workflow verify`, which still succeeds with exit 0; no refusal (the workflow is self-contained by decision Q6, and a refusal would make the snapshot worthless), no new verb (a read-only report belongs to `status`). The prompt hook would not mention it (a hook that names the catalog's change to an agent that cannot act on it is noise). The wiring is one small commit in `cmd/workflow.go` plus a golden once this is decided.
  - **Safety.** Tests and comparisons in temp roots (`mktemp -d` under `/tmp`, names without words the program matches on); HOME never reassigned for a test process (the `env -i` run sets it for the child only); the `piguard` guards stayed; no `pi`, `claude`, `codex` or `opencode` was started (`ps` shows none of mine and no leftover engine process; the `claude` processes it lists are the owner's sessions); no real workflow log, no `~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, `~/.pi`, `~/.config/opencode`, `~/.local/state/labdrian`, `~/.labdrian-overlay` and no Engram database was read or written by a test or a comparison; no background monitor. Slips against the tool rule, reported: three `cat` with a heredoc or `/dev/null` as input (no-ops), one `python3` with a heredoc (empty, a no-op), one `python3 --version`, one `sed --version` and one `sed -n` (the hook refused the command and nothing ran). The rest used `bat`, `rg`, `fd`, `sd`, the Edit and Write tools and script files.

  - **Owner decision (2026-10-10): catalog drift is surfaced as option A, and wired.** `workflow status` gains an optional `profile_drift` object (`kind` `changed` or `retired`, `profile`, `recorded_digest`; for `changed` also `current_digest` and the differing `fields`), absent when there is no drift; `workflow verify` prints one warning line on stderr and still succeeds with the same stdout and exit code; no refusal, no new verb, the prompt hook does not mention it (`TestProjectContextDoesNotMentionCatalogDrift`). Commits: `1197850` `feat(cmd)` (+341 -7 / 4: the field, the warning, `builtInProfileCatalog`, `profileDriftWarning`, `profileWorld.exec`, the tests); `a2adc80` the four recorded transcripts (data, 6 KB, recorded from the new program, read in their diff: the changed and retired cases show the field and the warning, the version 2 case without drift and the version 1 case show neither); `0f82d6d` the harness that replays them. New outputs, classified: (a) status and verify of a version 2 workflow whose recorded profile differs from or is absent from the built-in catalog, new by decision; the old binary reads such a log as foreign (the downgrade of the batch 28 entry, unchanged); (b) nothing else differs. Verification as in the fix round, run again after the last code commit.

  - **Consumer map: every package that imported the engram adapter, what it used, where it went.** `promote` (writer, page, eligible, explicit, propagate, sync): `Observation` to `memory.Observation`; `Deps.Engram *engram.Store` to `Deps.Memory promote.Memory`, a port promote owns made of `Lister` (`ListObservations`, used by `Sync` and `Plan`) and `HistoryReader` (`ObservationsIncludingDeleted` and `RelatedEdges`, used by propagation); `ObservationLookup` stays a function. `query` (query, gate, embedarm): `Row`, `Standing`, `MatchAll`/`MatchAny`, `SnippetBudget`, `SnippetAt` and `SearchTokens` to `memory`; `Deps.Engram` to `Deps.Memory query.Memory`, made of `Searcher` (`Search`), `StandingReader` (`Standings`), `CoverageReader` (`CoverageSnapshot`) and `DegradationReporter` (`Degraded`); `attachStandings` takes a `StandingReader` and the embedding arm a `CoverageReader`, so each function asks for what it uses. `skillstale`: `Observation` to `memory`; `Config.Store *engram.Store` to `Config.Observations skillstale.ObservationLister` (the `Lister`). `staleness`: `Observation` to `memory`, nothing else. `mcpserver`: `GetOutcome.Observation` to `memory.Observation` (its dependencies were already functions). `ops/testdata`, `ingest` (a comment): the type name. `cmd/longterm-mem` stays the one importer of the adapter (`engram.Open`, `*engram.Store` in `rundeps.go`, `exit_codes.go`, `project_resolve.go`, `engram_degraded.go`, the commands): the composition root. What moved into `internal/memory`: `Observation`, `Row`, `SearchResult`, `MatchAll`/`MatchAny`, `Neighbour`, `Standing` (with `Empty`), `Edge`, `SearchTokens` with its stopwords, and the snippet rule (`SnippetAt`, `SnippetBudget`, `TruncationMark`, now exported because the adapter's tests and the rule share it). What stayed in `engram`: the connection and its read-only fallback, the SQL, the row scans, the FTS5 `highlight()` offsets (`extract`, `matchOpen`/`matchClose`, `joinTokens`) and the accepted relation kinds: all of it SQLite. `vecindex` mentions the adapter only in a comment.
  - **What does not fit, said plainly.** (1) `memory.Standing` and `memory.Neighbour` keep their `json` tags, because `query.ResultRow` and the MCP query tool carry a `Standing` as it is and the wire format must not change; the alternative is a query-owned wire type and a mapping with no variation to justify it. A domain type that knows its JSON names is the one compromise; L4 (query-owned types) is the place to remove it if wanted. (2) `HasMemory` (the "established" question of project adoption) is still a method of the concrete store used by `cmd/project_resolve.go`; it belongs to the port L7 defines for adoption (`Established`), not to a reader of observations, so L1 did not touch it. (3) `exit_codes.go` (`engramReadable`) and `cmd_status.go` read the concrete store because they are the root. (4) `promote.Memory` and `query.Memory` are composites (one field in `Deps`), with the single-method ports as the types the functions take where they take one; a `Deps` of four fields for one store would only repeat it. (5) The domain package imports `strings` and `unicode/utf8` only.

The docs commit passive.
  - **Map of the vault, the clock and the action, and where each went.** *Vault:* `internal/promote` imported `internal/vault` in one place, `address.go` (`vault.Runner` to run `scripts/allocate-address.sh` under a 10 s timeout). The script path, the timeout, the trimming, the exit-code and the empty-output checks moved to `internal/vault/address.go` as `vault.AddressAllocator{Root}` with `NextAddress() (string, error)`; promote owns the port (`AddressAllocator`) and `Writer.Addresses`; `cmd/longterm-mem/rundeps.go` (`runPromote`, used by `promote` and the MCP tool) and `cmd_sync.go` wire the adapter. Other `vault` users in `cmd` (`Rebuild`, `Retrieve`, `Provisioned`, `PrerequisitePresent`) are unchanged: promote never used them. The allocation logic that stays in promote is the part that reads the vault's pages and the manifest (reuse of a page's own address, `recordAddress`), owed to L3. *Clock:* `nowFunc` had 5 readers: `EmitPage` (the created/updated day), `recordAddress` (the day a new manifest is seeded), `Writer.register` (the log entry), `writeSyncState` (the completion instant) and, through them, `Writer.Promote` and `Sync`. `EmitPage` and `Allocate` take the instant as an argument (the Writer reads it from `Writer.Clock`, `Sync` from the same writer); `Clock` is a port promote owns, `cmd/longterm-mem/clock.go` is the adapter (`utcClock`, the wall clock in UTC), and promote converts to UTC itself so the date rule does not depend on the adapter. No other `time.Now` is left in promote (the guard says so). The tests' `fixedNow` is a fake clock a test owns; no test changes shared state. A Writer without a clock is refused up front by `Promote` and `Sync` (`checkPorts`); one without an allocator is refused only when a promotion needs a new address, because re-promoting a page that has its own address never asks. *Action zero value:* see the next point.
  - **`ActionNone`: every place an action is set, before and after.** The kind is set explicitly in exactly three places and none depends on the zero value: `Writer.Promote`'s create branch (`Action{Kind: ActionCreated}`), `UpdateInPlace`'s success (`Action{Kind: ActionUpdated}`) and `skippedAction` (`Kind: ActionSkippedLocalEdit`). The zero value reaches a reader in two ways, and neither reached an output: `UpdateInPlace`'s three error returns (`Action{}, err`; `Writer.Promote` returns `Result{}` on any error before it reads the kind), and `Writer.Promote`'s ineligible return (`Result{}`), which the sync path never produces (`Sync` calls `Promote` only after `decidePromotion`, which has already required `Eligible`) and the explicit path cannot (`explicit=true` is always eligible). Consumers of `Action.Kind`: `cmd_promote.go` (compares with `ActionSkippedLocalEdit`, prints the kind of a result that came from a successful explicit promotion), `rundeps.go` (`promotionWrotePage`: created or updated), `mcpserver` (`Kind.String()` of the result of the explicit promotion). So the only value that changed meaning is the zero one, and the program never hands it to a consumer; the golden, the 13-step comparison and the unit tests agree. `promotionWrotePage` lost its `Page.Address == ""` guard, which existed to catch exactly the old zero value; its test gained a case (an unset action beside a page does not rebuild) that failed while `ActionNone` was last in the list.
  - **Pins first.** `TestPromoteGolden` runs `promote` and `sync` in process over a temp vault (a fixture allocator that counts, stubs for the index scripts), a temp HOME and a temp Engram database of four observations, for eight steps: an ineligible observation promoted explicitly, again, `sync --dry-run`, `sync` (two promotions), `sync` with nothing new, `sync` after a revision and a soft delete (an update and a propagated patch), `promote` over a page edited by hand (refused, exit 6), `promote` of an unknown id (exit 7). Each golden holds the arguments, exit code, stdout, stderr and every file of the vault (the pages, the log, the index, the manifest, the precedence sidecar with its hashes, the sync state), 36 KB in all; only the day the test runs (UTC) and the instants of that day are replaced by markers, so a clock that answered another day fails them. Recorded at `b2b7d4a` (identical code to `2795e97`; the batch's first commit only changes comments and a test comment), read, and unchanged at every later commit. Mutant: the page's day taken one day earlier fails all eight. The test skips when `python3` is not on PATH, as `exit_codes_test.go` does. `TestCmdPromote_WhenTheVaultCannotGiveAnAddress` pins the three failures of the allocator (exit non-zero, prints nothing, script absent) with the wording recorded from the old binary; both new `cmd` tests also pass on the untouched `2795e97` tree.
  - **RED, then mutants.** `ActionNone`: the tests failed to build (`undefined: ActionNone`); with the constant added last in the list they failed at run time (`the zero Action has Kind created`, `Promote ... Action:{Kind:created}` for an ineligible observation, `rebuild ran 1 time(s), want 0`); then first in the list, green. Clock: the tests failed to build against the old `EmitPage`; the UTC rule failed at run time while `EmitPage` formatted `at` as given (`created: 2026-08-01`, want `2026-08-02`) and passed with `.UTC()`. Killed: the adapter returning the epoch (5 failures, the unit test and four goldens), `Promote` and `Sync` without their `checkPorts`, the sync state without the clock, the manifest day without it, the log date shifted a year, the page date shifted a year (only after `TestPromote_DatesTheNewPageAndTheLogWithTheWritersClock` was added: the first set of unit tests missed the manifest day and the page day, and the golden caught the allocator one). Allocator: `internal/vault` tests failed to build; killed: no trim, the exit-code check ignored, the empty-output check ignored; promote side: the allocator's error swallowed, an empty address accepted, the missing-port check removed (a nil dereference). The guard: with the debt line left in, `TestArchitectureFollowsTheDependencyRule` named it as no longer a violation (the RED of the deletion); a file in promote importing `internal/vault` again fails it (`internal/promote -> internal/vault breaks the dependency rule`), and one calling `time.Now` fails it (`internal/promote -> time.Now ...`).
  - **Binary comparison.** The binary of `2795e97` and the binary of the tip over 13 invocations in throwaway worlds, each binary in a world built from nothing (a temp HOME under `env -i`, a temp vault with a counting allocator and stub index scripts, a temp Engram database made from the module's schema with `sqlite3` and four observations): `promote --id` (an observation that never qualifies, again), `sync --dry-run`, `sync`, `sync` again, `sync` after a revision and a soft delete, `promote` over an edited page (exit 6), an unknown id (exit 7), then the allocator failing (exit 3, two invocations: `promote` and `sync`), printing nothing, absent, and back. Compared: exit code, stdout, stderr, the list of files and the content of every file of the vault (the day and its instants masked). **Result: 13 of 13 identical**, no difference of any kind to classify: the exit codes (0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 6, 7, 1, 1, 1, 1, 0), the text (including the doubled `promote: promote:` the old binary already printed), every byte written. What the comparison cannot see: the MCP `promote` tool (its rebuild decision is unit-tested and its `Action` string is `Kind.String()` of an explicit promotion, which is never `none`), and a promotion that spans midnight UTC (the clock was read at each use before and still is; only the source of the time changed).
  - **Isolation.** Every `go` run and every comparison process ran with `HOME` set to a fresh directory under the scratchpad (a wrapper that also sets the XDG variables and names the Go caches), `GOPROXY=off`; the binaries ran under `env -i` with their own HOME and `LONGTERM_MEM_VAULT` and `LONGTERM_MEM_ENGRAM_DB` pointing into a temp world; the in-process tests set the same variables with `t.Setenv`. The proof that the HOME-resolved path is the temp one: a program writes the marker `b30-isolation-marker-7f3a9c` to `$HOME/.engram/engram.db` and reads it back from the path a program resolves (`os.UserHomeDir`), under the wrapper: it reported a home under the scratchpad, `under the owner's home: false`, marker found; it never opened a path under the owner's home. (The first draft of that program also read `/home/labdrian/.engram/engram.db` to show the marker was absent there; I caught it before running and rewrote it so that no path of the owner's is opened.) The owner's Engram, vaults, `~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, `~/.pi`, `~/.config/opencode`, the running `longterm-mem mcp` processes and Ollama (11434) were not touched; no `pi`, `claude`, `codex` or `opencode` was started and no test of this batch talks to an embedding server. `ps` after the last run shows none of my binaries and no world or home directory of the scratchpad in a command line.
  - **Verification (from `longterm-mem/`, temp HOME per process, `GOMODCACHE` and `GOCACHE` explicit, `GOPROXY=off`).** `gofmt -l .` empty; `go vet ./...` and `staticcheck ./...` clean; `go test -count=1 ./...` every package ok (20); `go test -race` on the root guard, `cmd/longterm-mem`, `promote`, `vault`, `ops` and `mcpserver` ok; `GOOS=darwin go build ./...` ok; `GOOS=windows go build ./...` fails in `internal/vault` (`Setpgid`, `Kill`) and `internal/vecindex` (`Flock`) exactly as on `2795e97`: this unit does not change it, the new vault file uses no system call, and promote, which used to build only if `vault` did, now has no dependency on it. A scan of every Go file changed since `2795e97` for private-use, zero-width and separator code points and a literal ellipsis finds nothing (the files written contain no `\u` escapes).
  - **Slips against the tool rule, reported.** Four commands were refused by the hook before they ran (a `cat` as the first command of three, and an `ls` in a build line); nothing ran. Several Edit calls failed on a mistyped path (`/home/labdrian-sdd-overlay-worktrees/...`) without effect, and a stray Write to `/home/` was refused by the system. `sd` with a multi-line or `\t` pattern silently did nothing in this environment (as noted in batch 29), so those edits went through the Edit tool.
  - **Decisions stopped on.** None needed. For the owner if he disagrees: (1) `Writer` is built by a struct literal, so a forgotten port is found at run time and refused with a message (`Clock` eagerly, `Addresses` when one is needed); a `NewWriter` with required arguments would find it at compile time, at the cost of every literal in the tests and an L8 shape (`Deps`) to settle first. (2) `AddressAllocator.NextAddress` takes no `context`: the adapter owns its 10 s timeout, as before, and `Writer.Promote` has no context to pass; threading one through `Promote`, `ExplicitPromote` and `Sync` is a change of API that L8 can make with the use cases. (3) The adapter's errors carry no package prefix and promote adds `promote:`, which keeps the operator's text byte for byte; the cost is that `vault.AddressAllocator` alone reads without a package name.
  - **What is left.** L2 is done. Open in longterm-mem: L3 (the `VaultRepository` port and `internal/vaultfs`, four slices), then L4 to L6.

## Next step

Batch 30 did L2 (promote owns `AddressAllocator` and `Clock`, the vault side implements the first and `cmd` wires both, `nowFunc` is deleted, `ActionNone` is the zero value of an action; and the two suggestions carried from batch 29). Next is L3 (the `VaultRepository` port and `internal/vaultfs` adapter, in four slices: the precedence store, the address map, the pages, the sync state, log and index; a single `VaultLayout`; ops `Doctor` and `Status` consume the port), then L4 to L6. Batch 29 did L1 (the `internal/memory` domain of longterm-mem, the engram adapter mapping rows to it, and the reader ports promote, query and skillstale own; the first unit of Phase G; and the suggestion carried from batch 28). Open: the decision on one reader for `.git` pointers (`gitprov`, `gitfs`, `skills/projectidentity`), C7 (quoting and the in-place upgrade of installed hook entries, Q1), the `atomicfile` unification decision for the propagate adapter, and the three `cmd` tests that depend on the temp path (see the batch 23 entry).
